The documented force-checkout recovery (content already upstream, verified by an empty two-ref diff of the suite paths, then a force checkout of the origin tip) cleared it exactly as prescribed — a fourth recurrence for the record, with the source fix still unable to clean a non-`main` served root.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-20, #2904 — the same non-`main` served-root recurrence: after the tester's two tests-commit persists the root spec checkout kept the eight suite files as unstaged modifications. The tester reported that `git checkout -- <paths>` was sandbox-denied despite the role's `git checkout*` allow rule, so the prescribed path restore was unreachable this round; the content was already upstream, so switching the root to `main` at teardown reconciled it (no content lost). A targeted `git checkout -- *` allow was appended for tester + developer via `set-permission`. Fifth recurrence on record.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-20, #2905 — sixth recurrence, same non-`main` served-root shape: the tester's `tests-commit` (4 suite files → `origin/main`) left those files as unstaged modifications in the root spec/2905 checkout. The prescribed teardown reconcile applied cleanly (content was already upstream on `origin/main`: `git checkout -f main` then fast-forwarding the local `main` ref to the API-written origin tip; nothing lost). Note `git checkout -- <paths>` was sandbox-denied for the SI too this run (the documented G-204 gap) — the force-checkout form worked.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-21, #2918 — seventh recurrence, same non-`main` served-root shape, on EVERY test-suite persist round (rounds 1–3, plus the round-2/3 suite persists): each persist left the suite files staged/modified in the root `spec/2918` checkout, and each time a branch switch (`git checkout main`, or a `-B` reset) was refused until the tree was cleared. The prescribed recovery (force-checkout the origin tip; content already upstream on `origin/main`) worked every time with nothing lost, and `git checkout -- <paths>` naming `.opencode/**` was sandbox-denied for the SI too, so the force form was the only reachable remedy.
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-19, #2897) — the block recurred after both tests-commit rounds; restoring the affected suite paths from HEAD before the branch switch cleared it exactly as the guardrail prescribes, so the remedy is sound (the underlying dirt remains a machine defect to fix at source).
- **source fix:** 2026-09-19 — `tests-commit` now best-effort reconciles the root checkout after the Contents-API write. **On `main`:** only when local `main` is fast-forwardable (never discards local commits) it `--mixed` resets onto `origin/main` (HEAD + index move, working tree preserved). **On any other branch** (the common case — the served root is on `spec/<N>` during testing): it STAGES the just-persisted suite paths (`git add`), so their content equals `origin/main`; HEAD is untouched, preserving serving-currency, and the staged entries clear on the next `git checkout`. This is what finally removes the "untracked/modified would be overwritten" block on the switch back to `main` after a `spec/<N>`-served test run (5th recurrence root cause: the earlier fix only handled a root on `main`). Root checkout only (skips linked worktrees); strictly no-op in mock mode (pinned by the harness assertion that `tests-commit` never emits `SYNCED:` offline). Verified empirically in a scratch repo for both the untracked and tracked-modified cases; `test-scripts.ps1` 112/112.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-21, #2915 — eighth recurrence, same non-`main` served-root shape on every suite persist (the planning→implementation persist and both tester rounds): the persisted suite files appeared STAGED in the root `spec/2915` checkout, and a later `-B` reset / branch switch was refused until the tree was cleared. The prescribed recovery (force-checkout the branch tip; content already upstream on `origin/main`) worked every time with nothing lost; `git checkout -- <paths>` naming `.opencode/**` remained sandbox-denied for the SI, so the force form was again the only reachable remedy.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-21, #2924 — ninth recurrence, same non-`main` served-root shape on both suite persists (the planning→implementation persist and the tester's `tests-commit`): the persisted suite files sat STAGED in the root `spec/2924` checkout. The source fix's staging behaviour now made the branch switch itself non-blocking (`git checkout main` completed, carrying the staged entries but NOT refusing), and the subsequent `git checkout -B main origin/main` reset cleared the index and left the tree clean — no manual path restore needed. `git checkout -- <paths>` naming `.opencode/**` was again sandbox-denied for the SI, but was not required.

### G-201: worktree_creation_not_idempotent_on_a_leftover_path
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-19
- **observed:** #2896 — after an interrupted run, a clean worktree for the issue still existed at the requested path; the developer's worktree-creation action failed with a raw git "already exists" error, and the developer had to verify the leftover's cleanliness and commit, then reuse it. The orchestrator had not swept leftovers before dispatching.
- **target_failure:** a resumable pipeline run hits a leftover worktree path and the developer dispatch fails on an opaque git error instead of proceeding, costing a step on every wave until the stale path is cleared by hand.
- **guardrail:** Worktree creation must be idempotent for an already-registered worktree at the requested path when it is clean and at the expected commit (reuse it), or fail with a named, actionable message. An orchestrator resuming after an interruption sweeps registered worktrees before dispatching a wave.
- **home:** .opencode/scripts/pipeline-state.rs (create-worktree) + .opencode/skills/pipeline-state/SKILL.md + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-19) — source fix applied: `create-worktree` now runs `git worktree prune`, REUSES an existing real worktree (a `.git` marker inside), sweeps an unregistered leftover dir, and retries once with the robust remover on a stubborn leftover. Pinned by two harness tests (`test-scripts.ps1`, 112/112): "sweeps leftover dir then is idempotent" and "reuses an existing worktree (.git marker)".

### G-188: ui_state_window_shorter_than_driver_cadence
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-18
- **observed:** #2892 round 1 — the tester could not capture the queued-waiting indicator's exact on-screen literal or the interrupt supersession live: the MCP driver's minimum send round-trip (~6 s per keyboard/interact call) exceeds the local companion generation window (~1–3 s), so every attempted send during a reply landed after `llm-done` and the queue window was sub-second. Round 2 closed both by driving the second send inside a SINGLE in-page async `execute_js` script (or a real-keyboard send against a deliberately long first generation), capturing the literal and the supersession within one window.
- **target_failure:** a live AC asserts a UI state whose window is shorter than the driver's round-trip cadence, so the state is never observed, the row reads UNVERIFIED, and a round (or the verdict) is spent on a harness-cadence limit rather than a product fact.
- **guardrail:** When a UI state's window is shorter than the driver cadence, drive the whole sequence in ONE in-page script (or widen the window with a deliberately long generation) rather than issuing sequential driver calls. If it remains unreachable after the widened lever, record a named lever-limited blocker with the shipped unit pin — never a bare UNVERIFIED and never a weakened assertion. A native-setter + `input` event does not reliably update React state after a bar clear; use real keyboard input or `execCommand('insertText')`.
- **home:** playbooks/tester.md (levers) + skills/dev-environment/SKILL.md (driver fidelity) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-18, #2892 round 2) — the single-in-page-script / long-generation lever captured both previously-unreachable legs (exact queued literal; interrupt supersession) live in one round.

### G-187: convergence_skips_control_unit_and_hook_cross_check
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-18
- **observed:** #2892 planning — the UI/UX Expert specified the reply-grace control in SECONDS with testid/id `companion-reply-leave-grace`, while the QA Expert's Plan rows and the seeded `settings` suites assumed a MILLISECOND field with testid `companion-reply-leave-grace-ms`. Both sections were internally consistent, machine-parsed literals were present in each, and the Architect's storage contract (integer ms) did not settle the DISPLAY unit — so a literal-presence check alone would have passed. The divergence was caught at convergence by cross-checking each control's unit + testid against the QA rows and the seeded suite rows; the SI realigned the QA section + suites and bound the hook in ST-6.
- **target_failure:** a plan's UI/UX control spec and its QA rows/seeded suites disagree on a control's display unit or hook name while each is self-consistent, so the developer builds one contract and the tester executes the other, burning a round on a contract mismatch the plan itself created.
- **guardrail:** At convergence, for EVERY new/changed control, cross-check the UI/UX display unit and the testid/id NAME against the QA Plan rows AND the seeded suite rows (not only that each artifact carries some literal); realign the contradictory sections in the same pass and bind the shipped hook in the owning sub-task. Storage-unit vs display-unit (e.g. persisted ms vs displayed seconds) must be stated explicitly on both sides.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (convergence) + playbooks/qa-expert.md (suite seeding) + references.md (this record)
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-19, #2899 — the UI/UX descriptor ids/labels, store hook names, and layer testid diverged from the Architect's API contract (and the QA rows referenced the layer generically), so a literal-presence check would have passed while the developer built one name set and the tester probed another. The SI cross-checked the hook/testid names against the QA rows and the seeded suite rows and realigned every section + suite in the same convergence pass, binding the shipped hook in the owning sub-tasks; no round was burned on a name mismatch.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-19, #2903 — the close intent was a NEW control binding decided at convergence: the close reply copy, the close skill name, the unchanged event/reply-surface names, and the two new formatters were cross-checked against the QA rows AND both seeded suites and realigned (the QA row that read "TBD at convergence" was bound char-for-char, and the seeded suite rows carrying pre-decision "PO-decline" wording were refreshed) before the plan was declared agreed; no round was burned on a copy/name mismatch.
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-18, #2892) — caught pre-implementation and realigned (QA rows + seeded settings suite + ST-6 hook binding); no round was burned on the mismatch.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-21, #2924 — the QA rows bound DOM hooks (`.fredo-window__surface`, `.fredo-window__header`, `.fredo-window__grip--{n,s,e,w,ne,nw,se,sw}`) that were cross-checked at convergence against the durable `window-manager`/`settings`/`launcher` suite rows before the tester entry; the round executed them with no selector mismatch, so no invented hook reached the tester.

### G-186: ark_number_input_id_override_breaks_display_sync
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-18
- **observed:** #2892 round 1 (live FAIL) — the reply-grace `NumberInput` kept displaying the raw typed text (`999999`, `-5`) with `aria-invalid="false"` while the persisted value clamped correctly (`60000`, `0`). Root cause: the component overrode the input's DOM `id` without passing Ark's `ids={{ input }}`, so the Zag machine's `getInputEl` resolved the generated `number-input:<machineId>:input`, found null, and its `syncInputElement` wrote nothing; the uncontrolled input kept the typed text while the React draft healed via the persisted-value effect. The shipped component test pinned only the persisted value + `aria-invalid` healing, so it could not fail on the defect. Round 2 fixed it with `ids={{ input }}` + a commit that writes the clamped seconds back to the draft, verified live (display `60`/`0`, invalid draft shows the error help).
- **target_failure:** an Ark/Zag compound input's explicit `id` override (without the matching `ids` prop) silently disconnects the state machine from the DOM input, so the controlled display never reflects the committed value; tests that assert only the persisted value/`aria-invalid` pass while the on-screen value is wrong.
- **guardrail:** When overriding a DOM `id` on an Ark/Zag compound input, pass the matching `ids={{ input: <id> }}` so the machine resolves the same element; when a commit clamps/normalizes a value, write the resolved value back to the local draft as well as persisting. A pin for such a control MUST read the RENDERED input value (not only the persisted value or `aria-invalid`) — the live DOM read is the authoritative oracle. Check the sibling controls in the same file for the identical override.
- **home:** playbooks/developer.md (Chakra/Ark input discipline) + playbooks/qa-expert.md (oracle honesty) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-185: live_nonfunctional_fail_dismissed_as_dev_artifact
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-18
- **observed:** #2893 round 2 — the latency bound failed live (window-present ~3 s against a ≤2 s bound, with a 2185 ms main-thread gap). The first read attributed it to the Mission Monitor mount in the Vite dev artifact; code + telemetry research showed the dominant cost was ordinary product code (the whole row store re-parsed several times inside the mount derive) that also runs in a packaged build, with the dev build only doubling it. The adopted fix was cost-only (parse each row payload at most once per row object) with an explicit output-parity proof.
- **target_failure:** a live non-functional failure is attributed to a dev-environment artifact and either dismissed or met by relaxing the bound, so a real product long task ships; or a performance fix changes behaviour because output parity was never asserted.
- **guardrail:** A non-functional live failure must be root-caused with a discriminating trace (source + telemetry) that separates dev-server effects from product code before any bound is reconsidered; the default is a product fix, and "dev artifact" is accepted only when the trace shows the measured quantity itself is dev-only. A performance fix must prove byte-identical output with a regression pin, and the bound is never relaxed to pass.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (fix-plan root cause) + playbooks/tester.md (verdict) + playbooks/developer.md (verification) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-184: convergence_leaves_placeholder_bindings_in_machine_parsed_artifacts
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-18
- **observed:** #2893 planning — the QA plan and the seeded suites were drafted before the Architect landed its bindings, so machine-parsed placeholders ("pending Architect confirmation", a placeholder CLI subcommand, a pending spike-artifact path) survived into the durable suites; the QA author had to be resumed to realign the requirement column and substitute every placeholder before the transition persisted the suites.
- **target_failure:** a parallel planning cluster whose sections land at different times leaves placeholders or stale "pending" notes in the machine-parsed artifacts (the plan's requirement column, the seeded suites), so the tester executes unresolved placeholders.
- **guardrail:** At convergence, before the plan is declared agreed, re-read the QA and UI/UX sections and the seeded suites against the Architect's now-landed contracts and substitute every placeholder or pending binding with the bound literal (command name, observable, bound). Do not declare convergence while a machine-parsed artifact still carries an unresolved placeholder.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (convergence) + playbooks/qa-expert.md (suite seeding) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-18, #2892) — the convergence pass re-read the QA section and the seeded suites against the Architect's landed contract and substituted every divergent literal (grace display unit + testid) before `## Convergence: agreed`; the tester executed the suites with no placeholder/stale-binding failure.

### G-183: forced_branch_switch_discards_si_tooling_edits
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-18
- **observed:** #2893 — the Self-Improver made in-domain pipeline-script and pipeline-doc edits mid-run, validated them with the harness, and then the required forced checkout to the tester-serving spec branch silently discarded every uncommitted edit; the fix had to be re-applied and re-validated later.
- **target_failure:** pipeline/tooling edits made by the Self-Improver during a run are uncommitted until the end-of-run doc-sync commit, so an intervening forced checkout (serving switch, spec sync) discards them with no signal and the validated fix never lands.
- **guardrail:** Before any forced checkout that can discard the working tree, preserve pending in-domain edits outside git (copy them under the issue scratch) or commit them; re-apply and re-run the validation harness before the doc-sync commit. A validated-but-uncommitted pipeline change is not landed.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (serving switch + doc-sync) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-19, #2897) — the SI staged its in-domain pipeline edits before the serving/branch switch and avoided a forced checkout while they were pending, so the validated changes survived and landed on main in the doc-sync push.

### G-182: served_webview_entry_missing_adapter_registration
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-18
- **observed:** #2893 round 1 — the skill-aware chat adapter was registered only in the standalone UI-library dev entry, not in the Tauri app's served webview entry, so the new companion path warned that it was called before the adapter was registered and silently no-oped. Every functional acceptance criterion failed live while the whole unit suite was green.
- **target_failure:** a frontend capability that registers through the app's adapter bridge is wired into only one entry point, so a served webview that loads a different entry never gets the registration and the capability is inert live despite green unit tests.
- **guardrail:** When a change adds or moves an adapter/bridge registration, identify EVERY entry point the app actually serves and register in each, mirroring that entry's established registration pattern; add a source-assertion guard that the served entry registers the capability, so a future removal fails a test rather than only a live round. Treat the entry you edited as unverified until the served entry is identified from the app's own HTML/config.
- **home:** playbooks/developer.md (verification) + playbooks/self-improver.md (dispatch review) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-181: on_the_go_improvement
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-18
- **observed:** #2893 round 1
- **target_failure:** (on-the-go pipeline improvement)
- **guardrail:** context-read guard false-positive on parallel same-role dispatches. The streak is keyed per actor, and a wave of N same-role agents each opens with one context read before any acts, so N consecutive reads accumulate. Limit 3 blocked the 4th concurrent developer in the #2893 wave-1 dispatch with a fresh legitimate session. Raised the context-read streak limit from 3 to 8, above the developer-count ceiling, documented in the state-machine, permissions and self-improver docs, and updated the harness guard test to 8 allowed plus the 9th blocked. test-scripts 106 of 106 passed.
- **home:** references.md (G-181)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-180: gh_image_extension_required_for_evidence_upload
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** the branch-commit evidence model was replaced by `gh image` user-attachments — `upload-evidence` now uploads a screenshot to GitHub and prints a `user-attachments` URL, committing nothing to the repo (the old model had accumulated 512 files / 38.6 MB on `main` and on every synced spec branch). The action therefore depends on the gh-image CLI extension and a `gh` token with push access to the repo.
- **target_failure:** the pipeline runs on a machine where the `gh-image` extension is not installed (or `gh` lacks push access), so every `upload-evidence` invocation fails and the tester's per-AC evidence never lands.
- **guardrail:** `gh-image` is a pipeline prerequisite: install with `gh extension install drogers0/gh-image` and verify with `gh extension list`; `gh` must be authenticated with push access to the repo (the gh-token route is used for a repo you can push to — no browser session/`GH_SESSION_TOKEN` is needed). `upload-evidence` fails with a named prerequisite error when it is missing; report that as a blocker rather than re-driving. The extension uses an undocumented GitHub upload endpoint — if it breaks, escalate to the Self-Improver as a tooling gap.
- **home:** skill: dev-environment (Evidence upload section) + skill: pipeline-state (upload-evidence action row) + playbooks/tester.md (evidence upload) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-179: plan_subtask_names_out_of_repo_evidence_input
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-18
- **observed:** #2888 — the Architect's implementation sub-task instructed the developer to record the recogniser's raw-output alphabet "as evidence" by sampling a token file from the downloaded model directory, which lives outside the repository. A developer following it would have probed an out-of-repo path (the G-172/G-009 family) for no load-bearing reason: the planned transform is input-casing-independent, so that corroboration was never required. It was caught in the SI's plan review and returned to the Architect, which replaced it with an in-app-only rule plus an explicit prohibition list. In the same review the Architect's verification sub-task carried a live-device leg ("ten spoken attempts during a real hold") that no agent can drive on this host. Separately, the plan's declared capital-vocabulary table listed one literal twice.
- **target_failure:** a plan's own sub-task names an out-of-repo file as an input (even "just for evidence"), or assigns a live-device verification leg to a developer sub-task, or declares a constant table with a duplicated literal — so the executing capsule hunts for an asset it cannot read or stalls on a lever it cannot drive, and the plan itself is the hazard rather than the dispatch brief.
- **guardrail:** Plan review MUST scan every sub-task for (a) any named input path not in the repository, (b) any verification leg that needs a physical device, a model artifact, an environment variable, or a tool absent from the executing role's sandbox, and (c) declared constant tables whose literal count does not match their stated distinct count. An out-of-repo path is removed or re-sourced from the app's own channels; a device/lever-dependent leg moves to the role that owns that lever or is restated as the deterministic sample plus a named blocker. "Corroboration only" is not an exemption — the executing agent cannot tell the difference.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (sub-task authoring) + playbooks/self-improver.md (plan review) + playbooks/developer.md (guardrails) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Partial (2026-09-19, #2897) — the hazard recurred in the live-probe/role dimension: a gating sub-task's decision output required a running managed server that the assigned role's sandbox cannot drive, caught at dispatch rather than at plan review; see G-205. The out-of-repo-path and constant-table dimensions stayed clean.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-21, #2915 — the constant-table dimension held: the plan declared a fixed pattern-count literal (8) that did not match its 10 named catalogue entries, and the SI's convergence scan caught and realigned it (to 10) plus the catalogue-size-vs-placement wording before implementation, so no round was lost.

### G-178: doc_consistency_row_cites_unread_content
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-18
- **observed:** #2888 — the tester's documentation-consistency row returned PASS, citing specific documentation locations (a heading plus a line range) and quoting a sentence-case wording. An independent search of the documentation tree found none of those phrases, and the cited line range is a different question entirely. Every product row in the same verdict was substantiated by live captures, DOM reads and raw numbers; only the documentation row's evidence was invented.
- **target_failure:** a tester row about documentation content PASSes on a plausible-sounding citation instead of the actual text, so a real documentation gap is reported as closed and the audit has to rediscover it by independent search.
- **guardrail:** A row that asserts documentation content MUST quote the exact lines it read (or attach a literal search receipt for the asserted phrase). The audit MUST independently verify any such citation before accepting the row — a paraphrase, or a line range with no quote, is not evidence.
- **home:** playbooks/tester.md (verdict evidence) + playbooks/self-improver.md (audit judgement) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-177: dead_owner_socket_survives_down_reclaim
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-18
- **observed:** #2888 — the environment's stop action killed the running instance, but two ports (the agent bridge and the OTLP HTTP receiver) stayed bound by an owner PID that was already dead; the reclaim loop could not free them because there was no live process left to kill. The next start therefore served the agent bridge on its fallback port and the OTLP HTTP receiver could not bind at all, while the OTLP gRPC receiver bound normally and ingested spans (visible in the startup log). A prior spec's committed evidence shows the same fallback serving a complete live round with valid evidence.
- **target_failure:** a port whose owning process is already gone survives the stop/reclaim path, so every subsequent live round runs on a fallback port with one receiver down, and the round is misread as a product or configuration failure.
- **guardrail:** Treat a dead-owner socket as an operating-system-level wedge, not a product defect. Before a live round, confirm from the startup log which receivers actually bound and which port the agent bridge took; a fallback bridge port plus a listening gRPC receiver with spans ingesting is a servable state — record it in the environment section and proceed. Free the socket by letting it age out or by ending the live handle-holder; never hunt for assets or loop implementation on this signature.
- **home:** .opencode/skills/dev-environment/SKILL.md (diagnostics) + playbooks/self-improver.md (pre-tester serving check) + playbooks/tester.md (environment section) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-176: on_the_go_script_fixes_apply_the_documented_remedies
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-18
- **observed:** #2887 — three documented remedies were still unapplied when the round needed them, and each cost real time before the SI fixed it in-run: (a) the guardrail that lets a developer obtain a worktree while a feature sits in `testing` (so the pipeline's own CI-fix path had no sanctioned entry); (b) the environment's restart action forwarded an empty spec id and therefore stopped the instance BEFORE failing its own argument validation, leaving the app down; (c) the environment's start action had no way to inject an environment variable into the launched app, so an env-gated verification seam could not be driven by any agent (fresh shell per invocation, shell chaining denied) and its rows became permanent named blockers.
- **target_failure:** a guardrail prescribes an exact script remedy, the remedy is never landed, and the next round that needs it burns a dispatch (or a round) discovering the gap again — the guardrail store then reads as "documented" while the pipeline still cannot do the thing.
- **guardrail:** When a guardrail's `guardrail:` text names a concrete script change, treat that change as a work item to apply in the SAME run it is observed (documented in the same pass and validated by the harness) rather than a note for later. Before dispatching agents that depend on a script capability, verify the capability exists (not merely that a guardrail mentions it).
- **home:** .opencode/scripts/pipeline-state.rs (create-worktree guard) + .opencode/scripts/dev-env.ps1 (Restart spec resolution, `-EnvVars` injection) + .opencode/skills/pipeline-state/SKILL.md + .opencode/skills/dev-environment/SKILL.md + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-18, #2887) — all three fixes landed the same run (the G-164 guard, the Restart spec resolution, and the `-EnvVars` passthrough), the harness re-ran at 104/104, and the CI fix then used the sanctioned worktree path.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-18, #2893 — the SI applied an on-the-go pipeline fix in the same run it was observed (the context-read guard false positive on parallel same-role dispatches): script + docs + harness regression updated together, validated at 106/106, and recorded through the improvement action.

### G-175: rework_loop_missing_forward_transition_blocks_the_verdict
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2887 round 2 — after a tester FAIL the SI dispatched the fix developer and then the round-2 tester WITHOUT re-running the `implementation → testing` transition. The feature therefore sat on its dev label while the tester drove the app, the machine derived round 1 for the round, and the machine's one-verdict-per-round guard refused the finished PASS verdict; recovery needed an SI transition followed by a manual draft flush, and the tester had burned a full round of work before the block surfaced.
- **target_failure:** the retry loop skips the transition back into `testing` before re-dispatching the tester, so the new round is never started, the verdict is stamped with the previous round, and a valid verdict is either refused or (worse) mis-attributed.
- **guardrail:** The rework loop is one cycle: architect fix plan → transition into `implementation` → developer work → **transition into `testing`** → tester. The SI MUST run the forward transition before dispatching the tester (the machine stamps the round from the count of testing entries, and a verdict may be posted once per round). If a verdict is refused for a round mismatch, do NOT re-dispatch the tester and do NOT hand-post the verdict: run the forward transition, then flush the draft.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (retry-round steps) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-174: local_build_receipt_from_shared_target_dir_is_untrustworthy
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2887 — the round-1/round-2 local receipts reported a clean clippy while CI failed the clippy gate. Two contributing facts: (a) local runs shared the repo-root build cache across worktrees, and those runs finished in seconds — the signature of a cache-freshness short-circuit rather than a real analysis; (b) the CI toolchain was NEWER than the local one, so a lint that exists only in CI could never appear locally. The eventual CI fix used a private target directory AND a positive control (re-introduce a known lint, watch it fire, then remove it) so the receipt proved the analyzer actually ran.
- **target_failure:** a build/lint/test receipt is produced from a shared, warm build cache and/or a different toolchain than the gate that will judge it, so the receipt is green while the gate is red — the round then burns on a CI failure that a trustworthy receipt would have caught.
- **guardrail:** A verification receipt for a gate must come from a build cache that is private to the work being verified, must state the toolchain version, and must include evidence the analyzer actually analyzed the changed code (e.g. a positive control that makes the check fail). A run that finishes suspiciously fast is not a pass — re-run it cold before claiming the gate is green. When the judging gate runs a floating toolchain, prefer the newest-documented idioms over newly-deprecated ones, and treat a local green as necessary-but-not-sufficient.
- **home:** playbooks/developer.md (verification) + playbooks/tester.md + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-17, #2887) — the private-target + positive-control receipt found and cleared the real CI lint on the first attempt.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-18, #2888 — the receipt came from a cold worktree with a stated toolchain, an explicit positive control (a stubbed projection failed 97 assertions before the restore re-ran green) and a suspicious-speed check; the judging gate went green on the first attempt.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-18, #2893 — every Rust receipt stated the toolchain, used a worktree-private target directory and a positive control (a forced-fail assertion or lint), and stated the no-Rust rounds explicitly; all CI checks passed on the first run of the spec PR.

### G-173: dev_env_up_fast_path_serves_the_stale_build
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2887 — twice, the serving instance was started before a new tip was pushed; re-running the environment's "up" action took its already-running fast path and reported the NEW serving commit while the process still served the OLD code. The first time, the tester would have tested stale code had it not been caught; the second, a forced stop/start was needed before the round.
- **target_failure:** the environment's "up" action reports the current branch tip while an already-running process keeps serving the previous build, so a live round silently verifies code that is not the tip and its verdict is void.
- **guardrail:** After ANY tip change, serve it with a full stop/start (never the already-running fast path), and verify what the process actually serves (the runtime's own status output plus at least one tip-identifying assertion) before dispatching a live round. Treat "the launcher says it is running" as unproven until the served commit is confirmed.
- **home:** .opencode/skills/dev-environment/SKILL.md + playbooks/self-improver.md (pre-tester serving currency) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-17, #2887) — the second occurrence was caught by the stop/start rule before the round.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-18, #2892 — `dev-env.ps1 -Action Up -Spec 2892` took the already-running fast path and reported the spec tip while skipping OTEL env injection; the SI forced a full Down → Up before each tester round (currency + OTEL for the live `telemetry_spans` receipt), and the testers served the true tip both rounds.

### G-172: missing_in_repo_fixture_triggers_out_of_repo_asset_hunt
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2887 round 1 — two developer capsules (the backend engine seam; the deterministic capture feed + WAV fixture) stalled by hunting for a real speech/audio test asset OUTSIDE the repo: a read of `~\fredo-models`, then `.wav` globs across `~\.cargo`, `node_modules` and `C:\Windows\Media`. Every out-of-repo read is sandbox-denied, so both capsules lost the round with no commit, no report and no gap recorded — the plan capsule asked for a fixture ("a 16 kHz mono WAV whose first spoken word is the assertion token") that cannot exist in-repo on a host with no speech asset. This is the third vector of the out-of-repo failure family (G-009): not a config path named by a brief, and not a self-provisioned tool, but a MISSING FIXTURE the capsule was told to produce.
- **target_failure:** a capsule must produce a test asset (media/audio/model/data fixture) that does not exist in the repo; the agent goes looking for one on the filesystem (`~`, the user profile, package caches, system media dirs), the sandbox denies it, and the capsule stalls silently instead of reporting the missing asset as a tooling gap.
- **guardrail:** A capsule that needs an asset absent from the repo MUST report a tooling gap (`block` with `--reason` naming the asset, why it is needed, and what was tried) — never probe the filesystem, package caches, or system directories for it. Dispatch briefs for any fixture-dependent capsule MUST state (a) the sanctioned in-repo lever or recipe that produces the fixture/evidence, and (b) the absence rule above, by name of the asset. Where a verification lever already exists that removes the asset need (synthetic event injection on the product's real channel, the real control plane, unit/static pins), the brief MUST prefer it and say so — a plan capsule demanding a recorded-media asset is a plan defect to raise, not a hunt to perform.
- **home:** playbooks/developer.md (guardrails) + playbooks/qa-expert.md (fixture recipes) + playbooks/self-improver.md (dispatch briefs) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-17, #2887 round 1) — both re-dispatched capsules completed green on the first attempt under the sanctioned-lever brief (ST-9 shipped the feed seam + an in-repo generated fixture; ST-1/ST-2 shipped the resident engine), with zero out-of-repo probes reported.
- **re-validated (plan-authoring gap exposed):** 2026-09-18, #2888 — every dispatch brief carried the prohibition and the sanctioned-lever list and no agent probed out of repo; but the Architect's OWN sub-task still named an out-of-repo model artifact as an evidence input, so the rule needed a plan-review gate, not only brief discipline. See G-179.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-19, #2897 — the planning cluster named the sanctioned in-repo deterministic capture-feed lever, every dispatch brief carried the prohibition, and the tester drove every audio leg (the committed 1.6 s fixture plus the deterministic over-limit variant) with zero out-of-repo probes. The lever itself needed a usability fix for the env-gated form; that is recorded separately as G-202.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-20, #2914 — every dispatch brief carried the in-repo L4 `FREDO_STT_FEED_WAV` lever + the absence rule; the tester drove every audio leg on the committed 1.6 s in-repo fixture and its deterministic 31 s variant with zero out-of-repo probes, and the acoustic leg was a named blocker rather than a hunt. The spec retired the `stt:transcript` content lever the playbook recipe named, so that recipe text was updated in the same pass — see G-219.

### G-168: spec_branch_forked_before_unpushed_local_base_pipeline_config
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2886 — the local base ref carried an UNPUSHED pipeline commit (agent-permission + dev-env hardening) while `origin/main` had advanced with the `tests-commit` side-effect commits. The spec-branch side-effect deliberately forks from `origin/main`, so `spec/2886` silently lacked that commit: the developer's worktree config was missing a granted verb and the serving root's dev-env script predated its port-reclaim hardening (the running app was found crashed on an orphaned socket at implementation entry). The SI had to reconcile the base (merge + push), sync the spec branch and re-create the developer worktree before any agent could run on the branch.
- **target_failure:** a spec branch is created from `origin/<base>` while the local base ref holds unpushed pipeline-config commits, so every agent on that branch runs on a stale sandbox/config and the config gap is misread as a product defect.
- **guardrail:** At run start (and before creating a spec branch or dispatching any developer/tester), confirm the local base ref is not ahead of its remote; if it is, reconcile and push the local commits FIRST, then create/sync the spec branch from the reconciled tip. A spec branch that already exists without those commits is synced and its developer worktree re-created before dispatch. Never assume the spec branch carries the local pipeline tip — verify the config files in the branch/worktree directly.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (pre-dispatch currency check) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-18, #2888) — verified at run start (local base ref equal to its remote) and the spec branch forked from the reconciled remote tip with the pipeline config already landed.

### G-169: animation_envelope_clearance_ignores_composed_transforms
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2886 round 1 — the plan bound a 14 px clearance as out-running the avatar's "≤5 px/side" motion envelope. Live sampling showed the separation collapsing to 8.00 px: the state animation composes a downward translate with a scale about the element's BOTTOM origin, which lifts the top edge by the translate PLUS the scaled height delta. The plan's envelope counted only the translate. The round-2 fix anchored a motion-invariant LAYOUT box (not the rendered rect) and added a motion reserve on the placement offset while keeping the bound as the accepted floor.
- **target_failure:** a UI clearance/separation asserted to survive animation is derived from an under-counted envelope (or sampled from the animated rect), so the live guarantee fails on ordinary animation frames while every static check passes.
- **guardrail:** When a clearance must hold through animation, derive the envelope from EVERY composed keyframe transform — translate AND scale about its transform-origin (a bottom-origin scale moves the top edge beyond the translate) — and anchor/sample the motion-invariant LAYOUT box rather than the rendered rect. Verify on at least one live frame whose measured transform matrix is non-identity, and state both axis envelopes in the constant's rationale.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (domain model / contracts) + playbooks/qa-expert.md (motion-frame sampling) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-170: keep_out_element_unmounted_makes_the_guarantee_vacuous
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2886 round 1 — the "the message never covers the app tiles" row could not be measured because the launcher unmounted the app-tiles grid at send and for the whole message display; the plan had required the grid's RESTING rect so a hide/collapse could not pass. The user-visible requirement (the tiles stay visible and clickable while a reply is read) was therefore violated and the keep-out was a no-op. Round 2 kept the grid mounted and measurable.
- **target_failure:** a "never covers X" requirement is checked against an element the product itself unmounts in the tested state, so the check passes vacuously or is unmeasurable while the user-visible requirement is violated.
- **guardrail:** Before claiming "never covers X", confirm X is present and measurable in the tested state; measure keep-outs against their RESTING rects, and treat a product path that unmounts X as a defect whenever the requirement says X must remain visible/usable — hiding or collapsing X is never a way to pass a keep-out row.
- **home:** playbooks/qa-expert.md (keep-out measurement) + playbooks/software-architect.md (domain model) + references.md (this record)
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-19, #2899 — the plan and the QA row required the "background never covers/intercepts" assertion with a present, visible, non-maximized window (a maximized window frame is opaque and fills the desktop), and the tester measured against that resting rect; the guarantee was non-vacuous.
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-171: pre_measurement_fallback_frame_below_bound
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2886 round 2 — the first rendered frame, before the first rAF footprint measurement lands, still renders the retained no-region fallback path and produced a single ~16 ms sample at 10.19 px against the 14 px bound (no overlap with the avatar). The tester disclosed it as an explicit observation with raw numbers instead of dropping it or relaxing the assertion; every per-reply-length state was comfortably above the bound.
- **target_failure:** a retained pre-measurement/fallback rendering path emits a single sub-bound frame; a strict per-sample bar fails a correct implementation, or a lax reporter silently drops it — either way the record misstates the guarantee.
- **guardrail:** When a retained fallback path can emit a pre-measurement frame outside the bound, record it as an explicit observation with raw numbers and judge it against the acceptance criterion's qualitative bar and the plan's sampling unit (per reply length); never silently drop it and never relax the assertion. Prefer a future round that anchors the fallback to the same bound-plus-reserve invariant.
- **home:** playbooks/tester.md (verdict disclosure) + playbooks/self-improver.md (audit judgement) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-164: create_worktree_blocked_during_testing_blocks_the_ci_flake_fix
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2883 — `ui-validate` failed on a documented G-156-class flake and the guardrail's own prescribed remedy is "land a scoped test-only fix on the open spec branch while in `testing`". The dispatched developer could not: `create-worktree` returns `BLOCKED: issue #2883 is not actionable (labels: testing)` (the guard accepts only `ready-for-dev`/`in-progress-dev`/`ready-for-test`). The developer isolated with an ad-hoc `git worktree add --detach` and reported the gap (G-142); the sanctioned `remove-worktree` cleaned up afterwards.
- **target_failure:** the pipeline's own remedy for a CI flake (a test-only fix on the open spec branch during `testing`) has no sanctioned worktree entry, so the fix is either not attempted (the issue blocks) or lands through an unreported ad-hoc worktree.
- **guardrail:** When the G-156 test-only fix path is used, `create-worktree` MUST accept the `testing` phase (label `testing`) for the issue — the fix changes no product code, so the phase's evidence guard is unaffected. Until the guard is extended, a developer landing such a fix reports the exact denial and the workaround used, and the SI records it. (Script change owned by the SI: accept `testing` in the worktree guard, documented in the `pipeline-state` skill + `state-machine.md`, validated by `test-scripts.ps1`.)
- **home:** .opencode/scripts/pipeline-state.rs (worktree guard) + .opencode/skills/pipeline-state/SKILL.md + docs/agentic-pipeline/state-machine.md + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-17, #2887) — the guard now accepts a `testing`-labeled feature; the round-2 CI fix was landed through a sanctioned worktree on the open spec branch instead of an ad-hoc `git worktree add`. Script change documented in the pipeline-state skill, state-machine.md and github.md and validated by the harness (104/104). Re-validated (2026-09-19, #2896) — a red Rust check on the open spec PR was fixed by a scoped test-only commit landed through a sanctioned worktree while the feature sat on `testing`; no round bump and no ad-hoc worktree.

### G-165: ci_flake_family_functional_tauri_internals_stub
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2883 — `ui-validate` failed with two unhandled rejections (`TypeError: window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__.invoke is not a function`) attributed to `FredoCompanion.crossWindow.test.tsx` while every assertion passed and the whole suite was green locally twice (1284/1284). Verified root cause: `CompanionContext` starts a **fire-and-forget dynamic `import('@tauri-apps/api/event')` chain** with no await/catch; the harness forced the Tauri branch with `__TAURI_INTERNALS__ = {}` and mocked the event module, so a continuation resolving after the file's teardown loaded the REAL module and threw inside an unhandled promise — vitest attributes it to the file and exits 1. Same class G-156 resolved for the seat-teleport test, in a sibling file.
- **target_failure:** a harness that forces the Tauri branch with an EMPTY `__TAURI_INTERNALS__` stub leaves any escaped real `@tauri-apps/api` call (emit/listen/unlisten) able to reject outside the test, so `test:run`/UI CI fails intermittently with 100% of assertions passing, and the failure is misread as a spec defect.
- **guardrail:** A test harness that forces the Tauri branch MUST stub `__TAURI_INTERNALS__` **functionally** (`invoke`, `transformCallback`, `convertFileSrc`, `metadata`) plus `__TAURI_EVENT_PLUGIN_INTERNALS__.unregisterListener` — never as `{}` — so an escaped real emit/listen/unlisten resolves harmlessly at every interleaving. An `await act(async () => {})` barrier does NOT settle a dynamic-import continuation and is not a substitute. Diagnose from the Actions job page's Annotations block (raw logs are auth-gated) and fix at source, test-only; never weaken an assertion, the gate, or an AC.
- **home:** references.md (this record) + playbooks/tester.md (CI-failure diagnosis) + the companion test harness convention
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-17, #2883) — the functional stub landed as a one-file test-only commit on the open spec branch during `testing`; the pre-fix stub reproduces the exact annotation, the new pin fails pre-fix, and `ui-validate` passed on the re-triggered run.

### G-166: intrinsic_measurement_floors_at_the_applied_box
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2883 round 1 (live FAIL) — a field that grows its height from a measurement never shrank: after clearing a long query the empty field still rendered 108 px against a 46 px intrinsic content height (bound 48 px). Root cause: the measure read `el.scrollHeight` while the element still carried its own applied clamp (`height: Npx`); `scrollHeight` is `max(clientHeight, contentExtent)`, so the read floors at the applied box, the mapping back to a height returns the same or a larger value, and the ladder is growth-only.
- **target_failure:** a component that measures its own content extent to size itself reads a box-dependent metric while that size is applied, so the measurement can never report shrinkage and the element ratchets up and stays there.
- **guardrail:** When measuring an element's intrinsic extent to size it, release the applied constraint for the read (`el.style.height='auto'` → read → restore the saved value verbatim, inside one rAF before paint) so the metric is content-only and the size mapping is reversible. Assert reversibility with a grow→clear→grow churn pin, and make the test oracle model the browser floor (`max(appliedClientHeight, intrinsic)`) — a stub returning a fixed number cannot fail on the broken implementation.
- **home:** playbooks/developer.md (measurement discipline) + playbooks/qa-expert.md (oracle honesty) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-17, #2883) — the clamp-release fix landed with an honest oracle (the pre-fix source fails the new pins `108px ≠ 48px`), the round-2 tester verified 48 px on clear plus exact 108→48→108 churn live, and no growth/cap behaviour regressed.

### G-167: interface_consumer_staffed_as_file_independent
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2883 — five concurrent wave-1 developers; the shell sub-task consumed props/types owned by two sibling sub-tasks (the bar's props and the bubble's props). To compile it had to declare them itself, then yield at rebase when the owners landed theirs. The plan's `requires:` edges covered only genuinely-sequential work, not interface-consumer → interface-owner.
- **target_failure:** a wave's "file-disjoint" workstream consumes an interface owned by a sibling still in flight; the consumer cannot compile until the owner lands, so it either declares the interface itself (duplication/conflict at merge) or stalls — surfacing as rebase churn instead of a planned ordering edge.
- **guardrail:** At decomposition, a sub-task that consumes a prop/type/module owned by another sub-task MUST declare a `requires:` edge on that owner (or the owner's interface MUST land in the same sub-task). "Different files" is not sufficient for wave independence — interface direction is.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (decompose — interface edges) + playbooks/self-improver.md (dispatch ordering) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Partial (2026-09-18, #2893) — a plan still declared a reply-push path on a capsule that only integrated after the bridge owner (a consumer→owner cycle), the exact pattern this record targets; the SI resolved it at dispatch time by folding the bridge into the consumer rather than re-planning, so the rule reduced but did not eliminate the recurrence.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-18, #2893 — caught by the SI's dispatch review, not by the plan itself; the plan's file lists had contradicted its own dependency edges.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-18, #2892 — the plan declared ST-3's `import type` on ST-1's context type while both were in wave 1; the SI's dispatch review had flagged the edge, and ST-3 rebased onto ST-1 with no local type fork (no rebase rework beyond the ordinary push race). The consumer→owner ordering remained a dispatch-review catch rather than a plan-declared edge.

### G-163: baseline_restore_contaminates_serving_tree
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2882 — after a BEFORE baseline leg (`dev-env.ps1 -Action Up -Spec 2882 -At 9e67a88`), the restore (`-Action Up -Spec 2882`) materialized the PRE-FIX file SET: it resurrected the two files the tested tip deletes (`CompanionListeningBubble.tsx` + its suite) and left them staged as additions, so the serving tree no longer matched the tested tip. The tester detected it, hard-reset the tree and verified it matched `origin/spec/2882`; the AFTER legs had already completed, so no verdict was taken from the contaminated tree.
- **target_failure:** a baseline (`-At <older-tip>`) leg leaves the serving tree on a mixed file set, so a later AFTER leg — or the verdict itself — is taken from a tree that is not the tested commit, producing a false PASS or a false FAIL.
- **guardrail:** After ANY baseline (`-At`) leg, and before serving or judging anything again: hard-reset the product tree to the tested tip (`git checkout -f`), confirm `git status --porcelain` is clean and every file the tip deletes is absent, and record the served commit in the report. A verdict may never be taken from a tree whose file set was not verified against the tip.
- **home:** playbooks/tester.md (baseline legs) + skills/dev-environment/SKILL.md + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-162: merged_tip_verification_must_grep_named_observables
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2882 — the plan bound an exact chip copy (`release Space to finish`) and an Enter-during-a-live-capture no-op (QA REQ-9 / QA-10). Neither existed in the implementation. The merged-tip verification checklist line (ST-9) caught it by grepping the plan's named observable strings against source and the shipped bundle BEFORE the tester round, and a focused in-flight fix landed it with no rework round consumed; without that pass the tester would have FAILed REQ-9 and burned a round.
- **target_failure:** a plan/QA row binds an exact user-visible string, testid or aria value, the implementation omits or renames it, and the gap is discovered only by the tester — a rework round spent on a one-line copy or guard omission a static grep would have caught.
- **guardrail:** Before the `implementation → testing` transition, a merged-tip verification pass MUST grep every exact named observable the plan/QA rows assert (chip copy strings, testids, aria values) against the source AND the shipped bundle, and route any absence back to the developer as a defect before the tester is dispatched.
- **home:** playbooks/developer.md (verification) + playbooks/self-improver.md (dispatch review) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-17, #2882) — the grep found the missing `release Space to finish` chip and Enter guard pre-tester; the focused fix landed on the branch and the tester's live round then verified the copy exactly, with no rework round consumed.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-18, #2888 — the merged-tip pass grepped all six named observables against source AND the built bundle before dispatch, and the live round then verified them exactly with no copy/guard rework.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-18, #2893 — the merged-tip pass grepped every named observable (the four exact reply literals, the CLI subcommand, the reply/live-region/surface testids, the event + command names, the reply-beat constant) against the spec-branch tip before the testing entry; all present, no copy/guard rework consumed.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-18, #2892 — ST-7's merged-tip pass grepped every named testid/id, copy literal, storage key and constant against BOTH the source and the shipped webview bundle before the testing entry; all present (the exported const identifiers are esbuild-minified in the bundle — the values/behaviour were corroborated), and the round-2 tester then verified the literals live with no copy rework.

### G-161: driver_keyboard_no_native_text_insertion
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2882 — the MCP webview keyboard tool injects keydown/keyup WITHOUT the browser's native editing action, so a synthetic press of a character key changes no input value; and a Control+Space chord arrives with `code:" "` (not `"Space"`), so a handler that matches the physical code cannot be driven by the tool at all. The tester recorded both as lever disclosures, drove CONTENT with the insert-text lever, judged INTERCEPTION from the app's own `defaultPrevented` decision plus unit pins, and used a correctly-shaped dispatched KeyboardEvent (its return value as the `preventDefault` oracle) for the chord.
- **target_failure:** a keyboard/typing spec's literal "the character lands" leg is judged UNVERIFIED — or falsely FAILed — because the driver's synthetic key press performs no native text insertion, or the chord cannot be delivered because the tool misreports the key `code`.
- **guardrail:** For text-entry acceptance, drive CONTENT with the insert-text lever and judge INTERCEPTION from the app's own decision (`defaultPrevented`, the handler's observed effect) corroborated by unit pins — and record the lever-fidelity gap as a named blocker, never as a product FAIL. For a chord whose handler matches a physical key code, verify both delivery and `code`, and fall back to a correctly-shaped dispatched event when the tool misreports it.
- **home:** playbooks/tester.md (levers) + skills/dev-environment/SKILL.md (driver fidelity) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-17, #2882) — the named-blocker plus decision-oracle scheme carried the affected rows honestly (no silent PASS) and the chord lever's return value proved the Ctrl+Space action in all driven contexts.
- **re-validated (scope broadened):** 2026-09-17, #2883 — the verdict disclosed that the MCP keyboard tool delivers **no native key events at all** to the webview (a synthetic `press` produced no Backspace deletion and no reliable Enter; only dispatched `KeyboardEvent`s reached the handlers), so the `Shift+Enter`/keyboard-scroll legs were scored by content-lever + dispatched-event `preventDefault` oracle + unit pins and recorded as named blockers. The gap is broader than the chord/insertion case this record first described; no product work was fabricated for it and no row was falsely PASSed.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-18, #2888 — the same two levers recurred (a `press` delivers no native text insertion, and the Space key arrives with a non-`Space` code so the real chord is undrivable). The round again scored CONTENT with the insert-text lever and INTERCEPTION from the app's own decision plus unit pins, and recorded both as named lever disclosures rather than product FAILs.
- **re-validated (scope broadened to activation/focus traversal):** 2026-09-21, #2924 — injected `Tab` did not move `document.activeElement` (it stayed on the first control) and injected `Enter`/`Space` on a focused window control did not dispatch its click, so live keyboard ACTIVATION of the chrome was undrivable. The round scored the tab ORDER, control roles, `tabIndex` and the non-modal frame from the DOM plus the in-repo unit pins, recorded the activation gap as a named driver blocker, and treated the global window shortcuts as deferred per the shipped baseline — no product work fabricated and no row falsely PASSed.

### G-160: post_transition_suite_edits_need_manual_tests_commit
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-17
- **observed:** #2882 — the machine persists the QA-seeded suites only on the FIRST `planning → implementation` entry (the G-159 hardening). In this run the QA Expert's reconciliation pass happened to land BEFORE that transition, so the persisted copies were complete; had it landed after (a normal cross-review/convergence outcome) the tester would have read stale suite rows from the served checkout, with no automatic path to persist the newer ones.
- **target_failure:** a durable-suite edit made after the first implementation entry (planner cross-review, QA reconciliation, tester extension) is never persisted, so the durable and served suites go stale and the tester executes superseded expectations.
- **guardrail:** After any durable-suite edit made once the feature is in `implementation` or later, the orchestrator MUST run `tests-commit` manually for the affected features before the tester reads them, and verify the persisted content on `main`.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (dispatch review) + playbooks/qa-expert.md + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-159: on_the_go_improvement
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-15
- **observed:** #2878 round 1
- **target_failure:** (on-the-go pipeline improvement)
- **guardrail:** Rework/rescope re-entry no longer re-runs tests-commit from a stale spec-branch tree. persist_tests now runs only on the FIRST implementation entry, so a spec branch whose .opencode/tests copies predate the tester's later extensions can never revert main's suite guidance (the G-038 hazard). Observed on #2878 round 1 to 2, the re-entry silently dropped 107 tester-extended lines from main before the fix, recovered from git history. Hardened pipeline-state.rs and documented in state-machine.md, test-scripts 100/100.
- **home:** references.md (G-159)
- **effectiveness:** Pending


### G-158: audit_accepts_pass_with_confirmed_ac_violation
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-15
- **observed:** #2877 round 1 — the tester's verdict was `Verdict: PASS` (18/22 rows) with 4 named-blocker UNVERIFIED rows and ONE confirmed edge defect: a duplicate start cleared the live capture indicator while the microphone kept recording (a reachable violation of the plan's continuous privacy invariant and an acceptance criterion). The QA Plan's own pass/fail criteria said "an edge case FAILing does not erase a primary PASS", so the PASS token mechanically cleared the testing-exit gate; only the SI's independent record-anchored judgment escalated it to a rework, which fixed it and re-verified PASS live in round 2.
- **target_failure:** a tester PASS verdict that carries a confirmed, reachable violation of a declared acceptance criterion (especially a continuous/privacy "never do X while Y" invariant) is accepted because the plan's edge-tolerance rule or the verdict token lets it through, and the defect ships.
- **guardrail:** The audit judges the evidence against the acceptance criteria, not the plan's edge-tolerance concession or the verdict token. A confirmed, reachable violation of a declared AC — particularly a continuous invariant ("never capture/show X without Y") — is a restart/rework even when the overall verdict is PASS and the named-blocker rows are legitimate. Do not conflate an acceptable environment-limited partial (a named blocker plus a unit/CI pin for an undrivable host lever, which ships documented) with a fixable product defect carrying a live repro (which loops).
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (audit judgment) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-15, #2877) — the PASS-with-confirmed-AC-violation was escalated to a rework; the fix landed and round 2 re-verified the invariant live on both origins, so the defect did not ship.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-17, #2882 — the audit judged the verdict ROWS against the effective ACs rather than the PASS token: the tester's 4 named-blocker partials are all environment/tooling-limited (no physical mic, undrivable model-removal, reduced-motion flip, network block, driver native-insertion) with unit/static pins, none is a reachable product defect, and all four continuous invariants (no indicator gap while capturing, no lost/converted space, no capture started by Ctrl+Space, no dictated phrase opening an app) were observed to hold. PASS substantiated; nothing escalated.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-17, #2883 — round 1's PASS-token did NOT survive independent judgement: the tester's verdict carried a decisive live defect (the bar field never shrank; a continuous short-content/typing-safety invariant violation) with a screenshot and an intrinsic-height oracle, so the SI audited it as a **FAIL** and drove a round-2 Fix Plan instead of accepting the token. Round 2 then verified the invariant live (empty field exactly 48 px, exact 108→48→108 churn) and the audit cleared it. The judge-the-rows rule is what stopped a false PASS from shipping.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-18, #2893 — the audit judged the round-3 verdict rows against the effective acceptance criteria rather than the PASS token: the two named-blocker rows (live ambiguity unreachable with one addressable app; no in-app open-failure lever) are environment/scope-limited with in-repo pins, and no AC row carried a confirmed reachable violation, so PASS was substantiated and nothing was escalated.

### G-157: on_the_go_improvement
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-15
- **observed:** #2877 round 1
- **target_failure:** (on-the-go pipeline improvement)
- **guardrail:** QA-seeded test suites were silently skipped: parse_feature_names() only matched a QA line that STARTED with '**Feature tests:**', so when the QA Expert wrote the declaration inside the QA policy blockquote ('> **Feature tests:** voice-input, companion, llama-setup') the planning -> implementation transition persisted ZERO suites and printed no warning. Hardened pipeline-state.rs to strip leading '>'/whitespace before matching, documented in state-machine.md, and pinned with a blockquoted regression in test-scripts.ps1 (100/100). Suites for #2877 were then persisted manually via tests-commit.
- **home:** references.md (G-157)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-15, #2877) — the rework re-entry (`testing -> implementation`) re-ran `persist_tests` against the blockquoted declaration and persisted all three suites with no manual step; the blockquoted regression is green in test-scripts.ps1 (100/100).


### G-156: on_the_go_improvement
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-14
- **observed:** #2876 round 2
- **target_failure:** (on-the-go pipeline improvement)
- **guardrail:** A known-flaky unrelated CI test has no in-sandbox recovery at merge time: pr_merge_guard blocks any non-exempt failing check (only sub-10s runner-provisioning failures are exempt) and the sandbox denies gh run rerun to the SI and tester, so FredoCompanion.seatTeleport (already documented in references.md as a re-run-passes flake) hard-blocked testing to audit despite rust-validate passing and the flake passing locally twice. Recovery applied: commit a real spec artifact (the references.md STT candidate-research section) to the spec branch to re-trigger the workflow on the same tree, which then passed. Proposed to the human: allowlist a scoped gh run rerun for the SI, or add a bounded one-shot flake retry to pr_merge_guard. **Resolved at source (2026-09-15, #2878).** The durable fix is not a rerun/retry bypass. The recurring failure was a test-harness synchronization gap: the companion teleport integration test's only pre-gesture barrier observed committed DOM, while its dispatch target was registered by a post-commit passive effect — so on the slower CI runner the gesture could fire before registration and exercise the host's no-entity fallback, leaving the assertion observing the wrong state. The human authorized a scoped, test-only fix round; the annotation on the public Actions job page identified the failure, the Architect traced it to the registration race (the sibling cross-window test already waited for its registration — this one had no equivalent), and the Developer landed a deterministic pre-gesture barrier with the assertion's intent and strength unchanged and no product-code change. The required check passed on the first run (`ui-validate`, 1m56s) and the spec PR merged. Lesson: when a known flake recurs, read the failing assertion from the job page's Annotations block, root-cause it, and prefer a bounded test-only source fix over repeated re-triggers — a re-trigger remedy is a stopgap, and its exhaustion is the signal to fix the cause.
- **home:** references.md (G-156)
- **effectiveness:** **Resolved (2026-09-15, #2878)** — see the resolution recorded in the guardrail above. History: Partial on #2877 (the flake recurred on both spec-branch tips; the documented re-trigger remedy worked but was needed twice). Escalated on #2878: the re-trigger remedy was **exhausted** — 4 consecutive spec-branch re-triggers each failed `ui-validate` on the same seat-teleport assertion (1m29s–1m54s) while the byte-identical app tree passed locally 1083/1083 (80 files), so `testing → audit` hard-blocked and the issue was blocked pending a human decision. The human then authorized the scoped test-only fix round; the root cause (a DOM-only pre-gesture barrier for an effect-registered dispatch target) was fixed at source, the assertion's strength was preserved, no product code changed, and `ui-validate` passed on the first run. The rerun/retry-bypass proposals are no longer required for this signature.
- **re-validated (class):** 2026-09-17, #2882 — `ui-validate` was green on its first run (1m52s) and the spec PR merged with no re-trigger needed. Locally, though, a sibling unhandled-rejection signature recurs in a DIFFERENT harness file (`FredoCompanion.crossWindow.test.tsx` via `CompanionContext.tsx`, 100% of tests passing with exit code 1, non-deterministic) — the same exit-code class that costs a full-suite re-run to clear. It did not block CI; effectiveness on the class stays Partial.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-17, #2883 — the sibling signature recurred and this time DID block CI: `ui-validate` exited 1 on two unhandled rejections in `FredoCompanion.crossWindow.test.tsx` (`CompanionContext` → real `@tauri-apps/api` emit) while every assertion passed and the same tree was green locally 1284/1284. The annotation was read from the public job page, the root cause was verified in-source, and the durable test-only fix landed on the open spec branch during `testing` (G-165) after which `ui-validate` and `validate` passed on the re-triggered head. The re-trigger remedy alone is NOT durable for this family.
- **re-validated (merge-gate queueing, new signature):** 2026-09-18, #2888 — the flake itself did not recur, but a burst of evidence commits (eleven rapid pushes to the spec branch) queued a chain of runs in the same concurrency group; the required aggregator check stayed unreported for ~25 minutes, so the merge guard read the PR as blocked. Waiting for the queued run to start resolved it — no bypass, no re-trigger needed, and the gate went green on the evidence head. A burst of evidence commits can therefore look like a hard block; check the workflow-run list before concluding the PR is unmergeable.
