### G-154: ui_ux_visual_verdict_cannot_measure_pixels
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-13
- **observed:** #2872 — the vision-capable UI/UX Expert's post-implementation verdict judged a +4px top / +8px right inset change. Because its sandbox denies shell, it could not run the pixel-measurement/zoom-crop pass it wrote; its findings rest on a direct vision read of the named frames calibrated by the tester's DOM numbers. The verdict is honest and PASS, but it cannot cite measured pixel clearances for a sub-10px delta.
- **target_failure:** a visual spec whose change is a small pixel delta relies on a design-role verdict that cannot measure pixels (no shell/image tooling), so the visual evidence is an estimated read rather than a measurement.
- **guardrail:** For a visual spec with a small pixel delta, the QA Plan's evidence MUST pair the tester's MEASURED rendered geometry (the authoritative numbers) with the design-role vision verdict (the qualitative read) — never the vision read alone. A design role's request for read-only image measurement (or a sanctioned shell action for it) is a tooling request to the Self-Improver, not a workaround to hand-roll.
- **home:** playbooks/qa-expert.md (visual evidence scheme) + playbooks/ui-ux-expert.md (visual verdict limits) + references.md (this record) + proposed human-owned sandbox addition

### G-153: visual_collision_evidence_frames_capture_one_surface
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-13
- **observed:** #2872 — the AC asserted the top-right clock/LED cluster never occludes a maximized window's titlebar controls. The captured maximized-window frames showed the controls unobstructed but did NOT include the cluster in the same frame, so the design verdict could only mark that criterion PASS on the rendered-geometry probe, not visually.
- **target_failure:** a visual spec asserts two surfaces do not collide/overlap, but the required evidence frames capture each surface separately, so the collision itself cannot be judged visually and the verdict must lean on indirect geometry.
- **guardrail:** When a visual AC asserts that two surfaces do not collide/overlap, the QA Plan's evidence scheme MUST require at least one frame containing BOTH surfaces in the exact state under test (e.g. maximized window with cluster and titlebar controls in one capture). Name that frame in the plan; the tester captures it and the design role judges the collision from it.
- **home:** playbooks/qa-expert.md (evidence scheme — collision frames) + playbooks/ui-ux-expert.md + playbooks/tester.md (screenshot conventions) + references.md (this record)

### G-152: in_js_wallclock_wait_wedges_webview
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-13
- **observed:** #2872 — the tester implemented a wall-clock wait (a 60s clock-advance check) as a synchronous busy-wait inside a webview JS evaluation. The runaway loop wedged the webview (bridge did not respond); the tester recovered and substituted the sanctioned bounded script sleep.
- **target_failure:** a tester implements a wall-clock wait/sleep inside webview JS; the evaluation leaves a runaway loop that wedges the webview/bridge, losing the leg and risking a false product FAIL or a stalled round.
- **guardrail:** NEVER implement a wall-clock wait/sleep in webview JS. Wait for a UI condition with the timeout-bounded webview wait tool, or for a wall-clock interval with a sanctioned bounded-sleep script; a JS busy-wait is a technique error (not a product defect) and its wedge is recovered through the standard bridge-recovery ladder.
- **home:** playbooks/tester.md (extends G-092 tool discipline) + skills/dev-environment/SKILL.md (recovery) + references.md (this record)

### G-151: mcp_bridge_wedge_mission_monitor_surface
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-13
- **observed:** #2872 — opening Mission Monitor in a live round blocked webview script execution (bridge did not respond within 7000ms, then request timeout after 2000ms) twice, surviving a driver-session stop/start; recovered only with a full dev-env Down → Up. Distinct from G-145 (Settings Telemetry) and the G-046 WS-handshake wedge. The tester worked around it by using the lighter Query Viewer window for all maximized-window probes.
- **target_failure:** a live tester opens a surface that wedges the MCP bridge, loses the driver mid-round, and either misreports the wedge as a product defect or burns the round.
- **guardrail:** Treat Mission Monitor as a known MCP-bridge-wedge surface alongside the Settings Telemetry section: expect the wedge, recover with a full dev-env Down → Up (not merely a driver-session restart), re-verify the clean handshake, and never report the timeout as a product defect or loop implementation for it. For window-geometry probes, prefer the lightest feature window that satisfies the AC.
- **home:** skills/dev-environment/SKILL.md (bridge-wedge surfaces) + playbooks/tester.md (driver recovery) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** **Confirmed** (2026-09-17, #2882) — the tester opened Mission Monitor repeatedly for the AC5/REQ-7 launch legs on the AFTER tip and the bridge did NOT wedge; the recovery ladder was known and no timeout was misreported as a product defect. (Separately, a stale socket from a pre-existing dead instance held :9223/:4318 and forced the bridge onto :9224 — an environment finding recorded in the verdict, not a wedge.)

### G-150: plan_decomposition_omits_qa_ui_requirement
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-13
- **observed:** #2871 — the QA Expert's REQ-15 and the UI/UX Expert's DR-6 (bar-level accessibility: an `aria-label` mode switch, an `aria-describedby` hint mirror, and one polite live region announcing once per event) were introduced by the planning cluster but owned by NONE of the Architect's ST-1/ST-2/ST-3 checklist lines. The ST-3 developer flagged the unowned requirement at completion; the SI folded it into the still-queued ST-2 at dispatch. No round was burned only because ST-2 had not yet run — the plan shipped with an unowned requirement the tester would otherwise have FAILed.
- **target_failure:** a requirement introduced by the QA Expert (a QA REQ) or the UI/UX Expert (a design requirement) — as distinct from a backlog acceptance criterion — is omitted from the sub-task decomposition, so it has no owning checklist line and surfaces as a testing FAIL or a scheduled gap rather than being implemented.
- **guardrail:** At convergence the SI MUST map EVERY QA REQ and UI/UX design requirement to an owning `- [ ]` sub-task line (with declared files), not only the backlog acceptance criteria — a requirement with no owning line is a plan defect returned to the Architect in the same pass. The Architect's decompose step MUST enumerate the QA REQ and UI/UX DR sets alongside the EARS/AC set.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (plan review) + playbooks/software-architect.md (decompose) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-13, #2872) — the convergence pass mapped EVERY QA REQ/NFR and every UI/UX design requirement to the single owning sub-task line (or a tester/QA-owned verification artifact) before `## Convergence: agreed`, and no requirement surfaced unowned at testing. (First observed #2871; caught pre-testing and folded into the queued sub-task.)
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-15, #2877 — the convergence pass mapped every QA Plan REQ (REQ-1.1…REQ-NF6), every UI/UX design requirement (DR-1…DR-12) and every EARS clause (R-1.1…R-5.4) to an owning sub-task line via explicit binding tables; the Architect's initial "1:1" claim was inaccurate and the SI returned it for an explicit QA-REQ/EARS → ST mapping before convergence. The F-38 rework kept the mapping intact and no requirement surfaced unowned at testing.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-17, #2882 — the QA Expert's coverage table bound every effective AC (body AC1-3, the PO-amended AC4-6, clarifications #1 and #2) AND every EARS clause R-1.1…R-7 to a REQ row and an owning sub-task line, and the Architect's decomposition carried an explicit AC→ST map (AC1→ST-4; AC2→ST-5; AC3→ST-5+ST-3; AC4→ST-4+ST-5; AC5→ST-1+ST-4; AC6→ST-1+ST-4; #1→ST-1; #2→ST-4; docs→ST-8; G-147→ST-9). The convergence pass confirmed both and no requirement surfaced unowned at testing.

### G-149: cross_slice_surface_extraction_strands_window_gesture
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-13
- **observed:** #2870 — extracting the interactive companion body into a new `CompanionEntity` rendered on two surfaces (the launcher home seat and the fixed away overlay) left the window-level Ctrl+right-click teleport gesture dispatching through a ref attached only to the overlay; at the home seat the overlay is not rendered, so the seat→away teleport was a silent no-op (a required AC path). The developer explicitly flagged the cross-slice wiring gap in its report; a focused wiring slice added a shared active-instance registry BEFORE testing, and the AC then passed live.
- **target_failure:** a component extraction that renders one interactive body on multiple surfaces leaves document/window-level gesture code dispatching to a single surface's handle, so the gesture silently no-ops on the other surface and the gap surfaces only in a live test.
- **guardrail:** When a sub-task extracts a component for multi-surface rendering, the plan MUST name who owns window/document-level gestures and how each surface's request reaches the active instance (a shared active-instance registry, a forwarded ref, or equivalent), and verify the gesture on EVERY surface before testing. A developer's explicit "cross-slice wiring concern" note is a PRE-TEST action item, not a retrospective entry.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (decompose — surface/gesture ownership) + playbooks/self-improver.md (dispatch review) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-13, #2870) — the gap was caught pre-test from the developer's note, fixed in a focused slice (+6 tests), and the seat→away path passed live in round 3. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2871) — the ST-1 per-window registry (`askActiveCompanion` → the active entity's `ask()`) routed a launcher-bar request to whichever surface is mounted (seat or overlay); the live round exercised the seat path with the shared handle and no dispatch gap surfaced.

### G-148: statically_checkable_a11y_nfr_needs_unit_pin
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-13
- **observed:** #2870 — the plan required the welcome bubble to be fade-only under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`; the implementation never called `useReducedMotion`. The live reduced-motion lever is not drivable on the host (the driver cannot flip `matchMedia`, and framer-motion caches the query even under a JS override), so the deviation survived three testing rounds and surfaced only as a static finding; the SI looped a round to gate it and add a product-unit pin.
- **target_failure:** a plan NFR that is statically checkable but whose live verification lever is undrivable relies on a non-drivable live leg, so a real deviation ships unnoticed until someone reads the source.
- **guardrail:** A plan NFR that can be asserted at the source/unit level (reduced-motion gating, token-native colors, no alpha-append onto `var()`, listener-once) MUST own a product-unit/static pin in its owning sub-task; the (possibly non-drivable) live leg is recorded as a named blocker ALONGSIDE the unit pin, never as the sole verification.
- **home:** playbooks/qa-expert.md (Non-functional checks) + playbooks/software-architect.md (decompose) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Partial (2026-09-17, #2886) — the named-blocker half held (the tester recorded the reduced-motion leg UNVERIFIED with the driver limitation named), but no product-unit/static pin for the reduced-motion gate was required by the plan or produced by the round, so the source-level half of this guardrail was exercised only by the tester's construction argument, not by a pin. Prior: Confirmed (2026-09-13, #2871) — the plan owned a product-unit/static pin for the reduced-motion cursor gate (the live `matchMedia` lever is undrivable, recorded as a named blocker) and the static CSS + unit pin cleared REQ-16; the #2870 remedy pattern held. (First observed #2870.)

### G-147: tauri_webview_build_missing_from_ci_parity
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-13
- **observed:** #2870 round 1 — the developer's CI-parity set AND GitHub CI covered only `pnpm --filter @fredo/ui typecheck/build/test` (which uses `apps/ui/vite.config.ts`, `@` → `apps/ui/src`), not the shipped Tauri webview build (`pnpm --filter @fredo/tauri build:webview`, `apps/tauri/vite.config.ts`, `@` → `apps/tauri/src`). A new `apps/ui` file importing via `@/` therefore built green locally and in CI but aborted the real app at Vite import-analysis (blank webview, no AC reachable) — a full tester round lost before any AC could be scored.
- **target_failure:** a frontend spec's verification gate covers the library build but not the actual shipped webview build, so a config-scoped module-resolution/bundling error ships and the app does not boot.
- **guardrail:** For any UI spec, developer verification MUST include the shipped Tauri webview build (`pnpm --filter @fredo/tauri build:webview`) in addition to the `@fredo/ui` checks; where feasible also boot-check the live dev server. In `apps/ui` production source use RELATIVE imports for shared modules — the `@/` alias resolves only under the `apps/ui` configs. Adding the webview build to `.github/workflows/validate.yml` + CONTRIBUTING.md is proposed to the human (human-owned infra).
- **home:** playbooks/developer.md (verification) + playbooks/self-improver.md (dispatch) + references.md (this record) + proposed human-owned CI addition
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-13, #2870) — the relative-import fix landed the app boot; every later round's verification ran `pnpm --filter @fredo/tauri build:webview` green and rounds 3-4 booted and passed live. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2871) — every round's developer verification ran the shipped Tauri webview build green and the app booted/served at each tip; no webview build failure. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2872) — the developer's round-1 receipt included the shipped Tauri webview build green (2551 modules) alongside the `@fredo/ui` build/test:run gates, and the app booted and served the tested tip; no webview build failure.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-15, #2877 — every developer round ran the shipped Tauri webview build green alongside `cargo check/test/clippy --locked` and the `@fredo/ui` build, and the SI re-ran it at the merged tip (2555 modules); the app built and served the tested tip via dev-env on `spec/2877`. No module-resolution or boot failure.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-17, #2882 — every developer round ran the shipped webview build green (2554→2557 modules) alongside `@fredo/ui` typecheck/build/test:run, the dedicated merged-tip verification ran it again plus `cargo check/clippy --locked -D warnings` on the untouched Rust tree, and the tester re-ran it on the served tip; the app booted and served every leg on `spec/2882 @ f076fa08`. No library-green/webview-red split.

### G-146: chakra_numeric_size_prop_resolves_to_token
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-13
- **observed:** #2870 round 2 — `EmptySeat.tsx` and the launcher seat wrapper passed `width={AVATAR_SM.width}`/`height={AVATAR_SM.height}` (bare numbers 80/100) to Chakra `Box`; Chakra v3 resolves a bare number as a theme `sizes` token, so `width={80}` → `sizes.80` = 320px (height landed at 100px only because `sizes.100` is undefined). The away placeholder measured 320×100 (AC3 FAIL) and the 320px wrapper pushed Fredo ~120px off the launcher centre axis.
- **target_failure:** a component passes a raw number to a framework size prop expecting pixels; the framework resolves it as a theme scale token, producing a wrong size that prop-level unit tests do not catch (no live geometry assertion).
- **guardrail:** For exact pixel geometry in Chakra v3, pass CSS unit strings (`"80px"`) — never a bare number (it is a token). Pin rendered geometry with a component/unit assertion on the RESOLVED computed size (`offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight` or computed style), not just the prop value; convert a shared numeric constant once (a `toCssPx`/`_CSS` helper) rather than repeating numerics.
- **home:** playbooks/developer.md (Chakra size props) + playbooks/qa-expert.md (rendered-geometry checks) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-13, #2870) — the unit-string fix + a resolved-geometry unit pin + a durable rendered-geometry row passed round 3 live (wrapper and placeholder exactly 80×100, centre-x on axis). Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2871) — the bar busy dot was specified and rendered as `6px` unit-string geometry (live DOM measured 6×6 px); no bare-number size-token regression.

### G-145: settings_telemetry_section_wedges_mcp_bridge
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2868 — selecting the Settings → Telemetry section wedged the MCP bridge: webview script execution and log reads timed out until a driver-session stop/start. No product error was logged and the section rendered correctly after recovery. Matches the pre-existing #2864 E-3 observation (the Telemetry section's full-viewport capture wedges the bridge).
- **target_failure:** a live tester that navigates to the Settings Telemetry section loses its MCP driver mid-round; unaware of the recovery, it reports the section — or the whole round — as wedged/failed.
- **guardrail:** After navigating to the Settings Telemetry section in a live round, expect the MCP bridge to wedge; recover with a driver-session stop/start and re-verify the section before judging. Never report the timeout as a product defect or loop implementation for it.
- **home:** playbooks/tester.md (driver recovery) + skills/dev-environment/SKILL.md (MCP driver staleness) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-144: concurrent_evidence_upload_race
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2868 — two evidence-upload calls issued in parallel raced the spec branch tip and one failed with a 409 (tip at one SHA but expected another); a sequential retry succeeded. The evidence model requires one upload per AC, so firing them together is the natural but unsafe pattern.
- **target_failure:** a tester uploading one screenshot per AC fires the evidence-upload commits concurrently; the branch-tip compare-and-swap rejects the later one and an un-retried upload silently loses an AC's evidence.
- **guardrail (SUPERSEDED 2026-09-17):** While evidence was committed to the spec branch, upload calls had to be issued SERIALLY — await each, confirm the printed URL, then the next; a 409 was retried singly, never batched. The branch-commit model was replaced by `gh image` user-attachments (no compare-and-swap), so concurrent uploads are now safe and this rule is retired.
- **home:** playbooks/tester.md (evidence upload) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** **Resolved** (2026-09-17) — `upload-evidence` now uploads via `gh image` to GitHub `user-attachments`; there is no spec-branch tip to race, so the serial-409 rule no longer applies. Historical re-validations: Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2870) — every round's per-AC evidence was uploaded strictly serially (`await` each, confirm the printed raw URL, then the next), across 3 upload rounds (7, 9, and the round-4 set); no 409 race occurred. The BEFORE set and each round's AFTER set used distinct directories/prefixes (`before-*`, `after-*`, `after-r3-*`, `after-r4-*`) per G-135. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2871) — both tester rounds uploaded per-AC evidence strictly serially with distinct round prefixes (`req*-01-*` round 1, `req*-*` round 2); no 409 race. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2872) — all nine BEFORE/AFTER evidence uploads were issued strictly serially (`await` each, confirm the printed raw URL, then the next) under distinct `before-*`/`after-*` prefixes (G-135); no 409 race.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-17, #2882 — the tester issued all eight evidence uploads (6 AFTER + 2 BEFORE frames) strictly serially (`await` each, confirm the printed raw URL, then the next) on `spec/2882`, under distinct `after-*`/`before-*` prefixes; no 409 race and every embedded URL is a raw spec-branch URL that renders.

### G-143: status_comment_draft_left_behind_repost_hazard
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2868 — posting a Status comment does not consume its body file. A conventional status draft from one capsule was still present when the next capsule drafted its report; a later agent posting Status WITHOUT an explicit body file would have silently reposted the stale body. It was avoided only because each developer passed a unique body file.
- **target_failure:** a posted Status draft persists under the issue's scratch folder, so a subsequent agent or replay that relies on the conventional path reposts a stale, already-superseded status onto the append-only timeline.
- **guardrail:** Every Status report MUST be written to a unique, agent/round-tagged body file and passed explicitly; never rely on the conventional status draft path. At cleanup the SI clears leftover status scratch files. (Candidate durable hardening: the posting action consumes only the conventional default draft, leaving an explicitly-passed file untouched.)
- **home:** playbooks/developer.md + playbooks/self-improver.md (cleanup) + references.md (this record) — candidate state-machine hardening
- **effectiveness:** Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2870) — every developer Status report was written to a unique agent/round-tagged file (`status-st1.md`, `status-st2a1.md`, … `status-fix3.md`) and passed explicitly via `--body-file`; no stale conventional `status.md` was reposted. The SI still cleared the leftover status scratch files at cleanup (the conventional `status.md` held the pre-planning amendment draft).

### G-142: worktree_file_deletion_has_no_sanctioned_path
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2868 — the retiring capsule had to delete two in-scope files and found no allowlisted deletion verb: remove/git-remove are denied for every non-scratch path and no dedicated delete tool exists. The developer unblocked with an inline interpreter deletion (an agent-authored workaround) rather than a sanctioned pipeline path.
- **target_failure:** a developer capsule whose plan requires deleting product files hits a sandbox with no sanctioned deletion verb, so the agent either stalls or invents an unrecorded workaround.
- **guardrail:** The pipeline MUST provide a sanctioned way to delete in-scope files in a worktree. Until the allowlist grants it, a developer that must delete a file reports the exact gap to the Self-Improver (never improvises a permanent workaround); the SI proposes the concrete allowlist addition to the human, who owns the sandbox config, and records the gap.
- **home:** references.md (this record) + proposed human-owned sandbox-allowlist addition
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-18, #2892 — a developer needed to remove a temporary probe test from a worktree; `Remove-Item apps/...` was denied (only `.opencode/*` removal is granted) and it completed the deletion with the narrowest available means (`git add` then `git rm -f`), reporting the gap per this guardrail. The gap persists; the proposed `git rm*` developer allowlist remains the remedy.
- **effectiveness:** **Confirmed open** (2026-09-17, #2882) — the gap recurred: the retire-the-companion-dictation slice had to delete two product files and no deletion verb is allowlisted (`git rm` denied; only `.opencode/*` removal is granted), so the developer completed the deletion with an interpreter command and reported the exact gap per this guardrail. The proposed allowlist addition stands: `git rm*` for the developer.

### G-141: docs_sync_must_fast_forward_on_prefetched_main
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2865 — the SI's guardrail/doc-sync commit was made on a LOCAL `main` ref that predated the spec PR's squash merge (the PR was merged earlier in the same run, but the remote-tracking ref was not re-fetched). The first `git push origin main` was rejected non-fast-forward; to land the guardrails the SI fetched and merged the origin tip by SHA, which introduced a MERGE COMMIT into `main` and triggered the remote's "must not contain merge commits / changes via PR / required status check" rule violations (bypassed, but the intended linear write was violated).
- **target_failure:** the SI commits the doc-sync/guardrail change on a stale local `main`, the push is rejected, and the recovery (merging the remote into `main`) writes a merge commit to the protected branch instead of a linear fast-forward.
- **guardrail:** Before the doc-sync/guardrail write, the SI MUST `git fetch origin` and base on the CURRENT `origin/main` tip FIRST (reset the local ref to the fetched tip before applying/committing the doc change), so `git push origin main` is a true fast-forward (one linear commit) — never `git merge` the remote into `main`. If a push is rejected non-fast-forward, do NOT merge the origin tip; re-fetch, move the pending doc change onto a `main` reset to the fetched origin tip (worktree-independent ref update), re-commit linearly, push.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (doc-sync step) + github.md (single-writer exception) + references.md (this record)
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-18, #2892 — after the squash merge the SI `git fetch origin`ed, reset the local `main` ref to the fetched `origin/main` tip (`git checkout -f -B main origin/main`), applied the ARCHITECTURE.md + FAQ.md patches, committed ONE linear change and `git push origin main` fast-forwarded (`446218f..ed30604`) with no merge commit and no non-fast-forward rejection.
- **effectiveness:** **Confirmed (2026-09-15, #2878)** — the SI applied the guardrail exactly: after the spec PR merged, it `git fetch origin`ed and reset the local `main` ref to the fetched `origin/main` tip (`9e67a88`) BEFORE applying the doc-sync/guardrail edits, then committed one linear change and pushed. `git push origin main` fast-forwarded (`9e67a88..4ddfeb9`) with no merge commit and no non-fast-forward rejection; the remote's PR/required-check rules were bypassed as the documented single-writer exception. Prior text: Pending (first observed #2865; the linear re-commit path is the candidate remedy).

### G-134: post_implementation_design_verdict_routing
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2864 (and re-validated by #2865) — a UX-audit spec requires a design-role visual verdict AFTER implementation, but the pipeline has no phase for a post-implementation design pass; the SI must route the design role back outside the phase model, and the verdict is not a timeline artifact of its own. #2864 flagged the gap; #2865 executed the routing: the SI dispatched the vision-capable UI/UX Expert twice (a BEFORE visual evaluation after the pre-fix capture, and a before-vs-after verdict after the fixed-tip capture), the tester carried the design verdict verbatim-in-substance with attribution into the single `## Tests Runs` comment, and the audit adjudicated AC-1/AC-4 from that carried evidence.
- **target_failure:** a spec whose acceptance requires a post-implementation design-role verdict stalls because no pipeline phase owns the design pass; the orchestrator either skips it (AC unmet) or improvises an unrecorded path (untraceable evidence).
- **guardrail:** For a spec whose AC requires a post-implementation visual/design verdict, the Self-Improver MUST schedule the design-role pass explicitly after the developer push: author the verdict to a named file under the issue's scratch directory, have the tester carry it verbatim-in-substance WITH attribution into `## Tests Runs` (the evidence the testing gate reads), and do NOT substitute a testid/geometry probe. Distinct BEFORE and AFTER artifacts are mandatory (see G-135). The design role never captures screenshots itself when its sandbox lacks the dev/webview tools — capture is orchestrated through the sanctioned environment path.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (post-implementation design routing) + playbooks/tester.md (carry the design verdict) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-12, #2864) — the design verdict (21 BEFORE + 24 AFTER frames, 11 improvements, dispositions F1–F13/H1–H9) was carried into the Tests Runs comment and adjudicated at audit. Re-validated (2026-09-12, #2865) — the UI/UX Expert read 31/31 BEFORE + 32/32 AFTER frames across dark/light/matrix/amber, the verdict named 11 improvements and 0 regressions with all 15 issues dispositioned, and the tester carried it; the audit cleared AC-1/AC-4 from that carried evidence. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2870) — the SI scheduled TWO design-role passes: a round-3 post-implementation verdict (5/6 items PASS) and a round-4 re-adjudication after the tester re-captured AC5 (verdict 6/6, the AC5 concern CLOSED). BEFORE/AFTER artifacts used distinct directories and prefixes (G-135); the verdicts were authored to named scratch files (`.opencode/tmp/2870/design-verdict*.md`) and summarized in the SI Summary (the single-round testing model could not retro-fit them into the already-posted `## Tests Runs`, so the SI carried them). The vision pass caught an evidence-quality gap (an AC5 frame that did not show the asserted Fredo) that the functional verdict had accepted on sampler data. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2872) — the vision-capable UI/UX Expert authored the AFTER verdict to `.opencode/tmp/2872/design-verdict.md` (PASS, criterion-per-row + a carriable summary) after the developer push; the SI used a TWO-PASS tester round (capture/upload, then resume the same session to append the verdict and flush) so the tester carried it verbatim-in-substance WITH attribution INTO `## Tests Runs` — closing the #2870 single-round retro-fit gap.

### G-135: before_after_evidence_shared_path_overwrite
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2864 — the BEFORE screenshot set and the AFTER re-capture were committed under the SAME evidence filenames, so the AFTER upload overwrote the BEFORE commits; representative BEFORE frames had to be re-uploaded under distinct names. #2865 prevented the class by using a distinct directory + filename prefix for BEFORE and AFTER from the start.
- **target_failure:** a before/after visual spec writes both passes to the same evidence path/names, so the AFTER pass destroys the BEFORE record and the before-vs-after comparison (and the audit) loses its baseline.
- **guardrail:** BEFORE and AFTER evidence in a visual spec MUST use distinct directories and distinct filename prefixes (a `before` prefix/dir and an `after` prefix/dir); never rely on upload order. The plan names both schemes, and the tester's evidence uploads repeat the distinct prefixes so the committed URLs are separately addressable.
- **home:** playbooks/qa-expert.md (evidence scheme) + playbooks/tester.md (upload naming) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-12, #2864 — the overwrite occurred, then was remediated by re-uploading under distinct names). Re-validated (2026-09-12, #2865) — 31 BEFORE frames (`before-*` under a `before/` dir) and 32 AFTER frames (`after-*` under an `after/` dir) stayed separately addressable through both capture passes and the verdict; no BEFORE frame was overwritten. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2872) — the BEFORE baseline (`before-cluster-geometry`) and the AFTER set (`after-cluster-*`, 8 frames) used distinct directories + prefixes in both the scratch tree and the committed evidence dir; the AFTER uploads could not overwrite the BEFORE record.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-17, #2882 — the BEFORE baseline (`9e67a88`) captured `set`-and-Enter / `monitor`-and-Enter frames under `.opencode/tmp/2882/e2e/before/` with the `before-*` prefix and the AFTER set under `e2e/after/` with `after-*`; both BEFORE URLs remain separately addressable in the verdict and the BEFORE/AFTER pair is what proves the fix.

### G-136: durable_suite_resolution_superseded_by_scope
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2864 — a durable suite carried a prior binding resolution that a later spec's scope contradicted (a state recorded as an accepted residual / follow-up that the new spec brought in scope). #2865 hit the same shape: the theming suite pinned the semantic-token bridge gap as "pre-existing; follow-up scope", and the companion/settings suites recorded the not-ready-gate text contrast as an "accepted residual" — all three superseded when the backlog brought that defect in scope. The QA Expert reconciled every affected suite in the same planning pass (new extension rows; historical PASS/FAIL records preserved, none deleted).
- **target_failure:** a durable feature suite carries a prior resolution that a later spec's binding plan supersedes, so the tester would execute stale expectations and the suite would contradict the plan it is meant to verify.
- **guardrail:** At planning, the QA Expert MUST read the durable suites for the feature domains in scope and RE-RECONCILE any prior resolution the plan supersedes (a residual brought in scope, an "out of scope" annotation, a frozen contract that changed) — append an extension row/note in the same pass, preserving all historical PASS/FAIL records (never delete or rewrite them). The orchestration/plan review verifies the reconciliation is recorded.
- **home:** playbooks/qa-expert.md (suite reconciliation) + playbooks/self-improver.md (plan review) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-12, #2864 — the stale resolution was surfaced and reconciled). Re-validated (2026-09-12, #2865) — the QA Expert reconciled theming E-12/F-14, companion F-45 and settings F-7 as superseded and added the #2865 extension rows across all four domains in the planning pass; the tester ran against the reconciled suites with no stale-expectation failure. Re-validated (2026-09-12, #2868) — the settings/launcher/desktop-chrome/theming/window-manager/companion suites all pinned the retired modal-or-gear container; the QA Expert superseded those rows with #2868 extension rows in the SAME planning pass (historical PASS/FAIL preserved, none rewritten) and the tester executed the reconciled suites with no stale-expectation failure. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2870) — the QA Expert reconciled the companion/launcher/window-manager/desktop-shell suites whose rows pinned the removed `!companionPresent` render gate (the #2853 hide-while-visible model superseded), appended #2870 extension rows (F-59..F-68, S-20..S-23, R-33..R-36, launcher S-14/R-35/R-36, window-manager S-12, desktop-shell S-9/R-13) with all historical PASS/FAIL preserved, and the tester executed the reconciled suites with no stale-expectation failure. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2871) — the QA Expert reconciled the launcher/companion/llama-setup suites (the "command bar is a grid filter only" resolution superseded for the ACTIVE-companion state) and appended #2871 extension rows with history preserved; the tester executed the reconciled suites with no stale-expectation failure. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2872) — the QA Expert reconciled the `desktop-chrome` suite in the planning pass: F-17/S-8 (the shipped symmetric `top:16/right:16` record, with the `|diff| ≤ 6px` tolerance that had let it pass against the 20/24 intent) was SUPERSEDED by #2872 extension rows F-25..F-29 / S-9 / R-22..R-23 / E-19..E-20 asserting the exact asymmetric 20/24, all historical PASS records preserved; the tester executed the reconciled suite with no stale-expectation failure.

- **re-validated:** 2026-09-15, #2877 — the QA Expert appended the #2877 extension rows and supersession notes to the `voice-input` / `llama-setup` / `companion` durable suites in the planning pass (history preserved), the tester refreshed the F-38 row to PASS plus a round-2 log, and no stale prior resolution contradicted the plan. No stale-expectation failure surfaced. (The initial `**Feature tests:**` declaration was silently skipped because it sat inside the QA policy blockquote — see G-157 — not a G-136 issue.)

The QA Expert marked every affected row `G-136 SUPERSEDED (#2905)` (history preserved, none deleted), added the `#2905 extension` rows, and the product invariant test's static-only leg was inverted in the developer's ST-4; the tester executed the reconciled suites with no stale-expectation failure.

### G-137: accent_filled_control_thumb_must_contrast_track
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2864 — the vision pass caught, before the tester ran, an accent-filled control whose thumb did not contrast its accent track under a non-default accent (the thumb inherited the track's hue). #2865 made it a named pre-tester check and the tester measured the filled thumb vs the live accent track.
- **target_failure:** an accent-filled slider/switch thumb is rendered in (or near) the accent hue of its track under a non-default accent, so the control's position is unreadable even though the surrounding surface passes contrast checks — a non-text (graphical object) contrast failure that only a visual/accent pass catches.
- **guardrail:** Any accent-filled control with a thumb/track or range/track pair MUST be visually checked AND measured under a non-default (pale and hue-distinct) accent: the thumb/range vs the track MUST clear the non-text contrast floor. The QA Plan carries this as an explicit pre-tester case, not a discover-during-audit item.
- **home:** playbooks/qa-expert.md (non-functional checks) + playbooks/tester.md (accent measurement) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-12, #2864 — the pre-tester vision pass gated it). Re-validated (2026-09-12, #2865) — the tester measured the filled thumb vs the live accent track at 5.38:1 and the on-accent label at 5.38–13.88:1 across dark/light/matrix/amber; the check cleared before the tester round completed.

### G-138: visual_matrix_cell_reachability_unverified
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2865 — the plan froze a 17-state AC-1 capture matrix, but at capture time nine cells proved structurally unreachable through the real product on the host: the llama.cpp "missing/installing/error" states cannot render because the readiness resolver falls through to the machine's PATH/winget shim (it always resolves installed); no unknown-size stream exists for the indeterminate-progress cell; the launch watchdog is unreachable because a real launch becomes healthy in a few seconds; the all-installed wizard summary is superseded by the gate. The pass recorded each as a named blocker with the attempted repro, and the audit accepted them as PO-anticipated partials — but the reachability question should have been settled at planning, not discovered at capture.
- **target_failure:** a visual/state-matrix spec freezes a required state list without verifying each cell is reachable through the real product, so the capture pass discovers unreachable cells mid-run and the AC ships as a partial with unplanned gaps.
- **guardrail:** When a plan freezes a required state/evidence matrix, the planning pass MUST confirm a real-product reach recipe for each cell (no stubs/scratch) BEFORE the matrix is frozen. For any cell that is structurally unreachable on the target environment, obtain a plan-level disposition in the same pass: either a deterministic real-product test affordance owned by the plan, or an explicit named-blocker acceptance carried by the PO (recorded per the named-blocker discipline). Never let a required cell's unavailability be discovered for the first time during capture.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (state-matrix plan) + playbooks/qa-expert.md (reach recipes) + playbooks/self-improver.md (plan review) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending (first observed #2865). Applied (2026-09-12, #2868) — the planning pass identified that the AC-3 "no feature exposes a settings section" state is structurally unreachable live in the shipped app; the Architect owned a deterministic component-test affordance in the decomposition (registry stubbed to expose zero settings sections) and the live leg covered the ≥1 case, so the cell was never discovered as a capture-time gap. The planning-time disposition (per this guardrail) held; effectiveness re-classification awaits more specs.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-17, #2883 — the plan's small-window legs were written at `700×900`, BELOW the product's own shipped floor (`tauri.conf.json`: `minWidth 900`, `minHeight 600`), so AC3's "largest surface that fits" and the edge legs would have been scored against an unreachable size. The Architect traced the config in review and bound **900×600**; the QA Expert re-keyed every affected row and the durable-suite rows in the same convergence pass, and the tester scored the live legs at 900×600. No unreachable cell reached capture time.

### G-139: framework_default_semantic_token_shadows_declared_value
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2865 (root-caused from the #2864 residual) — the theme defined several semantic color namespaces as flat dotted keys, which the framework emitted as escaped-dot custom properties, while the framework's own default config defined the same names as NESTED objects whose dash-form custom properties won the token resolution. The declared theme values were therefore unreachable at runtime for every name that collided with a framework default: small text resolved to the framework's stock muted color (a 1.53:1 contrast failure) and the summary surface resolved to a stock near-white box. Declaring the overlapping namespaces in the same nested shape as the framework default made the theme value win; live measurement then confirmed the intended values.
- **target_failure:** a theming layer declares a semantic token whose name already exists in the framework's default config using a shape that cannot override the default, so components resolve the framework's stock value (or an empty value) instead of the theme's — producing off-brand surfaces and contrast failures that token-source inspection alone cannot detect.
- **guardrail:** For every semantic token name that also exists in the framework's default config, declare it in the SAME NESTED shape the framework uses (not a flat/suffixed key), and verify the emitted custom property resolves to the theme value AT RUNTIME (computed style), never merely that the source declares it. A source-level token guard that asserts the resolved value is the right regression pin; a component's color must be checked in the running app.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (theming-contract token selection) + playbooks/tester.md (runtime token verification) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-12, #2865) — the nested-shape repair made the declared values win; the audit's live reads showed the colliding names resolving to the theme values (no stock/empty), the not-ready gate card rose from 1.53:1 to 7.36:1 (dark) / 16.3:1 (light), and the token guard (plus a refreshed sibling emission assertion) pinned it.

### G-140: transient_state_screenshot_capture_is_thread_bound
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2865 — capture of short-lived UI states depends on the webview JS thread (the screenshot path falls back to an in-page canvas render): a busy-wait freeze that holds the state blocks capture outright, while a timed freeze is followed, on release, by overdue timers repainting the next state BEFORE the frame is grabbed. The server-launch "starting" middle phase (an ~800 ms window) could not be captured after three attempts; the first and last phases and other transients were captured via a synchronous freeze (trigger + busy-wait in the same task) or a naturally long-lived state. The capture pass recorded the cell as a named blocker rather than fabricating it.
- **target_failure:** a visual evidence pass burns attempts and still cannot capture sub-second transient states because the capture primitive needs the same thread the transient is animating on, so a required matrix cell is silently dropped or misrepresented.
- **guardrail:** For a discrete transient that renders before its scheduled timers run, use a synchronous freeze (trigger + busy-wait in the same task) and keep the freeze short; for a naturally long-lived in-flight state, capture without a freeze. Record any genuinely uncapturable transient cell as a named blocker with the attempts made — never substitute a representative frame. The durable fix is a capture primitive that grabs the last-painted compositor frame while the page thread is blocked (or a documented hold seam); propose it to the human as a tooling change rather than hand-rolling one.
- **home:** playbooks/tester.md (transient capture technique) + playbooks/self-improver.md (tooling-gap routing) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending (first observed #2865; a hold/capture primitive is the candidate durable fix)

### G-128: external_identifier_assumed_from_product_name
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-11
- **observed:** #2855 round 1 — the plan and an AC described a one-click `winget install llama.cpp`, and the developer pinned the winget PackageIdentifier to the product name (`llama.cpp`) alongside the exact-match flag. No winget package carries that identifier; the canonical id is `ggml.llamacpp`. Live winget returned "No package found matching input criteria.", so the install action could never reach `installed` and the tester FAILed AC3 — a full rework round for a one-constant defect. The fix was the identifier plus a guard test.
- **target_failure:** a spec's implementation depends on an external package-manager identifier (or CLI invocation) assumed from the product's display name rather than verified against the package registry; the exact-match install/query fails only at runtime and burns a tester round.
- **guardrail:** when an AC's observable depends on an external package identifier or install verb, the planning pass (or a Phase-0 diagnostic sub-task) MUST verify the canonical identifier against the package registry and the plan MUST pin the exact command. Implementation must never infer an identifier from a product's display name; a constant/invocation guard test pins the verified identifier. This is the external-dependency analogue of the payload-field live-verification rule.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (Domain Model / API Contracts — external identifier verification) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-12, #2856) — the plan pinned the external dependency BEFORE implementation (the three files' Hugging Face revision `66a399f6…`, exact byte sizes, and SHA-256, verified live against the HF tree), implementation used the pinned manifest verbatim, and the real wizard pull downloaded all three files with in-place SHA-256 matching the pins. No identifier/URL/size defect surfaced through implementation or testing.

### G-129: tester_fail_rework_retry_reason_missing
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-11
- **observed:** #2855 — after a tester round-1 FAIL, the rework transition posted `## Fix Plan (round 2)`, but the re-dispatched agents' context block showed `Retry reason: (recorded reason unavailable)` and the developer's `Attempt:` labeled the producing round one low. The retry reason is derived solely from failed audit-verdict events, so a tester-FAIL rework (which has no audit event) never carries the missed-AC list the playbook promises. The work proceeded correctly only because the dispatch brief inlined the Fix Plan and the round-1 verdict explicitly.
- **target_failure:** a re-dispatched agent on a tester-FAIL rework receives no retry reason (and an off-by-one round label), so it cannot self-locate the missed ACs from its context block; the orchestrator must compensate in every brief or the agent may re-do passed work.
- **guardrail:** when the context block's retry reason is absent/unavailable on a rework dispatch, the orchestrator MUST inline the latest FAIL verdict's expected-vs-actual and the machine-posted Fix Plan in the dispatch brief. The durable fix (proposed, not yet applied) is to derive the retry reason from the latest tester FAIL verdict / Fix Plan when no failed audit-verdict event exists, and to align the producing round's label.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (retry dispatch) + .opencode/scripts/pipeline-state.rs (`retry_state` — proposed) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Partial (2026-09-12, #2856) — the tester-FAIL rework's context block again carried no derived retry reason; the SI compensated exactly as this guardrail prescribes (inlined the round-1 verdict expected-vs-actual + the machine-posted `## Fix Plan (round 2)` in the developer brief) and the rework completed with no re-do of passed work. The durable fix (derive the retry reason from the latest tester FAIL verdict / Fix Plan when no failed audit-verdict event exists) remains unapplied. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2870) — on the round-2 rework the developer's context block reported `Attempt: round 1` / a stale previous phase; the SI inlined the round-1 FAIL verdict + the authored Fix Plan in every retry brief (and a `## Fix Plan (round N)` was posted), so no passed work was re-done. The durable fix remains unapplied. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2871) — both rework dispatches' context blocks again showed `Attempt: round 1` and `Retry reason: (recorded reason unavailable)`; the SI compensated by inlining the machine-posted `## Fix Plan (round 2)` and the round-1 FAIL expected-vs-actual in every brief, and no passed work was re-done. The durable fix remains unapplied.

### G-130: scale_independent_stub_misses_stream_stall
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2856 round 1 — the plan's QA section deliberately required "no real multi-GB download" and drove the resume leg with a KB-scale local stub server. That fixture warmed the response body and never reproduced a real stream's idle window, so the pre-fix defect (the engine recomputed the resume SHA-256 over the entire on-disk prefix BETWEEN reading the response headers and the first body poll, letting the peer reset the idle stream after a few MB) passed the stub-based design and surfaced only when the human-directed REAL wizard pull resumed a ~1.3 GB partial. The round-2 fix (seed the hasher before the GET) was then verified on the same real transfer.
- **target_failure:** a streamed/resumable transfer behavior is tested only with a fast, scale-independent fixture (a KB-scale stub that never stalls idle), so a defect that depends on real transfer timing/volume — an idle-body window, a peer reset, resume-offset math — is invisible to the QA Plan and reaches testing/audit undetected.
- **guardrail:** For any AC covering a long/streamed/resumable transfer (downloads, uploads, replay drains), the QA Plan MUST include at least one leg that reproduces the real timing/volume class — a real transfer, or a fixture with a deliberately idle/slow stream and a partial at a realistic offset — never only a fast small stub. State the fixture's scale/idle behavior explicitly; a resume test whose fixture never idles cannot verify a resume-to-completion AC.
- **home:** playbooks/qa-expert.md (Required test data / fixture design) + playbooks/self-improver.md (plan review) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Re-validated (2026-09-12, #2857 round 3) — the plan's streamed-class leg (F-39) drove a real ~703-`llm-token`/703-distinct-cumulative-length generation over the product `llm_chat` transport (TTFT 2.83 s, total 8.97 s), sampling incremental deltas across time rather than one final block; the streamed class was visible and PASSed live. Note: the `≥20 s` alternative was unreachable on this host (`max_tokens=1024`, ~89–115 tok/s) and was declared as a named environment/UI-shape limit rather than substituted with a static claim.

### G-131: binding_methodology_override_residual_partial
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2856 — a binding human directive superseded the plan's QA methodology (real wizard-driven E2E only; the stub server + manifest override banned). The orchestrator recorded it as a `Status` amendment and inlined it in every dispatch. The tester re-verified the primary AC live, but the directive also removed the only sanctioned lever to force a genuine per-file network/server error, so one AC's error-surfacing was UNVERIFIED live that round; the tester carried it as a named-blocker row (round-1 live evidence on the unchanged surface + the new exhaustion unit test) and the orchestrator documented it as a PO-methodology-limited partial instead of looping a round with no available mechanism.
- **target_failure:** when a human/PO binding directive forbids the methodology a plan's QA section relies on, affected ACs become unverifiable live, and the loop either burns rounds re-attempting an impossible fixture or silently claims a PASS with an UNVERIFIED row.
- **guardrail:** When a binding human directive supersedes the plan's QA methodology, the orchestrator MUST (a) record it as a `Status` amendment and inline it in every dispatch; (b) map its effect per AC before the round; (c) for an AC whose only live lever the directive removed, keep it UNVERIFIED-with-named-blocker carrying its residual substantiation (prior live evidence on the unchanged surface + a unit/CI pin) and document it as a PO-methodology-limited partial in the verdict and audit rather than looping an un-drivable fixture; (d) never present that residual as fresh live verification.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (dispatch + audit partial handling) + playbooks/tester.md (named-blocker row) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Re-validated (2026-09-17, #2882) — two binding human clarifications that closed the PO amendment's remaining open items were recorded as a `Status` amendment BEFORE planning (top-ranked ambiguous match; a dictated transcript stays Fredo-bound including after editing) and inlined verbatim in every planner brief, the dev fixes and the tester dispatch; the affected rows carried named blockers with pins (REQ-5 ii, REQ-12 network/RSS, REQ-13 reduced-motion, REQ-15 native-insertion) rather than freshly-claimed live PASSes.

### G-132: human_authorized_preverdict_rework_stranded_by_evidence_guard
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2857 — a binding human directive added scope to a feature whose `testing` entry had been aborted by the human before any tester verdict (the testing runs were killed), and required the dev work to precede testing. The rework leg `testing → implementation` is gated on tester evidence (a `## Tests Runs` comment), which could not exist yet, so the authorized dev task was stranded — and the developer cannot even obtain a worktree while the issue label is `testing` (`create-worktree` requires a dev label). A prior orchestrator reconciliation ("test first, then rework") was explicitly overridden by the human.
- **target_failure:** a human-authorized scope addition arrives while the feature sits mid-`testing` with no verdict; the machine's testing exit guard offers no legal dev-first path, so the orchestrator either disobeys the human (burns a tester round just to manufacture a verdict) or bypasses the machine (unrecorded dev work, corrupted round accounting).
- **guardrail:** Use the machine's narrow `transition --to-phase implementation --human-authorized --reason "<directive + added scope>"` for exactly this case: it bypasses ONLY the `testing → implementation` evidence exit guard and records an auditable `human.authorization` event. Use it only when a binding human directive adds scope to a verdict-less aborted testing round; record the authorization and added scope as a `Status` comment; the Architect's authored `fix-plan.md` still posts as `## Fix Plan (round N)`. The flag is ignored on every other leg and without a non-empty reason. The machine change is in-domain (SI owns `pipeline-state.rs`), documented in the same pass, and harness-tested.
- **home:** .opencode/scripts/pipeline-state.rs + docs/agentic-pipeline/state-machine.md + playbooks/self-improver.md + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** **Confirmed** (2026-09-12, #2857) — the human overrode a test-first reconciliation ("first act is a DEV task, NOT testing"): the machine's `testing` exit guard required a `## Tests Runs` comment that could not exist yet. The `transition --to-phase implementation --human-authorized --reason "<directive + added scope>"` override was used live, recorded a `human.authorization` event, posted the authored `## Fix Plan (round 2)`, and the dev-first rework then completed through round 3 to PASS with no tester round burned to manufacture a verdict. `test-scripts.ps1` 99/99.

### G-133: reference_process_argv_switch_syntax_unvalidated
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-12
- **observed:** #2857 rounds 1–3 — the human's reference launch config (and the frozen AC1 contract/plan derived from it) carried `--kv-unified 1`, but that switch is a value-less boolean in the target binary; the trailing value becomes a stray argv token and the process dies at argument parse, leaving a 28-byte log. The product faithfully reproduced the reference, so every live launch failed until a rework round. Tester round 2 discriminated it live by value-tracking (`1` → `invalid argument: 1`; `0` → `0`), and the Architect verified the human's own target build tag rejects a value too. Round 3 corrected the emitted form to the bare switch and PASSed.
- **target_failure:** an AC/reference launch config copied verbatim from a human's example (or a tool-generated `.bat`) contains an argv form the target binary rejects — a value on a boolean switch, an unsupported token, a renamed flag — so the plan freezes the bad form as the AC observable, the product reproduces it exactly, and every live launch leg fails until a rework round finds and corrects the syntax.
- **guardrail:** When a plan freezes an external process's argv/config contract from a reference file, validate each flag's arity/syntax against the TARGET binary's own parser or help output (or the pinned upstream tag's argument table) at planning — never assume the reference is runnable. A boolean switch takes no value; an enum accepts only its documented tokens. If the reference and the binary disagree, raise the exact correction as an explicit AC amendment for human/PO approval rather than silently changing the observable.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (Domain Model / API contracts) + playbooks/self-improver.md (plan review) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

The skill now points agents to the sanctioned color-mix tint helper, documented in the same pass. Lesson: a skill's cached guidance can drift behind the canonical AGENTS.md rule, so skill text must be checked against AGENTS.md before agents copy it.

### G-125: behavior_change_omits_existing_unit_test_refresh
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-11
- **observed:** #2854 round 1 — the plan changed a user-visible status contract that an existing product unit test already asserted. No sub-task owned refreshing that test, so the dispatched developer had to edit the test file to satisfy the required build/test gate while the dispatch said "do not modify tests" — an unowned test edit that escaped the declared file ownership and the sole-test-author boundary. The SI reviewed the diff (two stale expectations refreshed to the AC-mandated behavior; no weakening, disable, or removal) and accepted it, but the plan gap forced the conflict.
- **target_failure:** a spec changes a behavior asserted by an existing product unit test, but the plan's decomposition omits the test refresh — forcing the developer to either violate "never modify tests to make them pass" or leave the required gate red, and the edit lands outside every declared file set.
- **guardrail:** At planning, the Architect greps the existing test suites for expectations that assert the behavior the spec changes and either folds the refresh into the owning sub-task's declared files or assigns it to the QA Expert as the sole test author. A developer may refresh an existing expectation to the spec-mandated behavior ONLY when that refresh is explicitly in scope; the SI reviews any such diff for weakening (the test-mutation rule) and records it.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (Decompose) + playbooks/self-improver.md (step-7 push review) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

- **re-validated:** 2026-09-12, #2865 — the semantic-token bridge repair changed the emitted token custom-property names; the developer refreshed three stale emission assertions in the theme-provider test to the corrected names with IDENTICAL asserted values, and the SI reviewed the diff for weakening (none) before the tester round. No behavior-changed-but-unrefreshed test surfaced.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-12, #2868 — retiring the settings modal invalidated the theming guard hard-pinned to it; the plan explicitly owned the relocation as its own capsule (declared files), the developer moved the guard to the new shell preserving the literal scan and every still-applicable token assertion (replacing only two modal-Dialog-chrome tokens that legitimately disappear), and the SI reviewed the test diff for weakening (none) before testing. No stale test import or behavior-changed-but-unrefreshed test surfaced. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2872) — the Architect's G-125 grep audit found ZERO product unit tests asserting the corner-inset constant, so the plan correctly owned no product test-refresh sub-task (the durable-suite F-17 tolerance tightening was assigned to the QA Expert as sole test author); no unowned test edit surfaced and no stale expectation blocked a build or the live round.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-13, #2870 — the spec changed behavior asserted by existing product unit tests (the removed `!companionPresent` seat gate; the corner-default position; the overlay gate). The Architect folded the refreshes into the owning sub-tasks' declared files (`CompanionContext.test.tsx`, `CompanionSettingsPanel.test.tsx`, `FredoCompanion.devMode.test.tsx`, `featureRegistry.dedupe.test.tsx`) and handed the DURABLE suites to the QA Expert as sole author. The SI reviewed each modified test diff for weakening (none — expectations refreshed to the spec-mandated behavior, none deleted/disabled/loosened). No stale test blocked a build; a one-off `FredoCompanion.seatTeleport.test.tsx` flake passed on re-run in isolation.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-17, #2882 — the plan changed user-visible contracts asserted by existing tests (the Ctrl+Space cascade, the exact-full-name Enter match, the dictated-exact-name launch). The Architect declared the supersessions and the exact test files in the owning sub-tasks, and the developer's report NAMED each re-pointed assertion (ten in `launcherVoiceDictation.test.tsx`, the hint-gutter math in `launcherCommandBarVoice.test.tsx`, the shell harness's `stt_check_model` answer) with the disposition per assertion. No assertion was deleted or silently weakened and no build/test gate was blocked by a stale expectation.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-17, #2883 — the plan superseded user-visible contracts pinned by existing tests (the fixed-size reply/`208×268` game card, the `SpeechBubble` geometry, the bar's single-line geometry and the reserved-gutter pins). The Architect declared the supersessions and the exact files per sub-task, each developer NAMED the pins it re-pointed, and the round-2 fixes re-pointed the measurement oracle rather than the invariant — keeping every assertion's intent and making the new pins fail on the pre-fix source. No unowned or weakened test edit surfaced.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-20, #2914 — a large removal changed contracts pinned by `tests/voice_invariants.rs` (16 rows) and ten UI test files. The Architect declared a per-assertion disposition table (keep / re-point / delete→replace) in the plan, each developer NAMED its re-pointed pins, and the final sweep added the UI-source legs the backend sub-task could not assert; the tester independently confirmed no assertion was deleted without a named replacement and none was disabled or skipped. No unowned or weakened test edit surfaced.
