- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-122: planning_transition_blocked_by_dirty_seeded_suites
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-10
- **observed:** #2852 — the `planning → implementation` transition aborted: `persist_tests` had already pushed the QA-seeded `.opencode/tests/**` to `origin/main` via the Contents API, but those same files were left dirty in the main working tree while the local `main` ref still lagged `origin/main`, so the transition's `git checkout -b spec/<N> origin/main` refused to overwrite them ("Your local changes would be overwritten"). Re-running the transition then re-ran `persist_tests` and added 8 tree-identical duplicate commits to `main`.
- **target_failure:** the `planning → implementation` transition cannot create `spec/<N>` because worktree-coupled branch creation (`git checkout -b`) collides with the planners' dirty seeded suite files; a re-run duplicates the tests-commit.
- **guardrail:** Create the spec ref with a worktree-independent ref operation (branch from `origin/main` without checking out) — never `git checkout -b`. The machine must NOT reset the worktree to force the checkout (it must not discard uncommitted work). After a transition is re-run following a partial failure, check for duplicate tests-commit commits. Recovery idiom if a manual spec-branch checkout still hits dirty seeded suites: remove the dirty tracked suite paths, fast-forward local `main` to `origin/main`, then re-check out.
- **home:** .opencode/scripts/pipeline-state.rs (`ensure_spec_branch`, hardened 2026-09-10; harness 98/98) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** **Confirmed** (2026-09-10, #2853) — the `planning → implementation` transition created `spec/2853` on the first attempt via the worktree-independent ref operation while the QA-seeded suite files sat dirty in the working tree; no checkout collision, no duplicate tests-commit. The hardening is validated live. Re-validated (2026-09-11, #2854) — `spec/2854` was created first-attempt via the same worktree-independent ref operation while the QA-seeded `companion` suites sat dirty in the main working tree; no collision, no duplicate tests-commit. Re-validated (2026-09-11, #2855) — `spec/2855` was created first-attempt via the same worktree-independent ref operation while the QA-seeded `llama-setup` suites sat dirty in the main working tree; no collision. Caution re-confirmed on a rework re-entry: the `testing → implementation` transition re-ran `tests-commit` after the tester had already pushed the same suite content, adding extra (content-convergent) commits to `main`. Re-validated (2026-09-12, #2857) — three branch syncs (post-dev, post-tester, pre-audit) each hit the tester's dirty `.opencode/tests/llama-setup/*` in the root working tree (already persisted to `main` by `tests-commit`); the SI cleared them with `git checkout -f` (the harness denies `git checkout -- <path>`) then fast-forwarded `spec/2857` and merged `main`'s tip SHA before pushing — no stale suite reached any tester entry, no clobber, and the rework `testing → implementation` transition again re-ran `tests-commit` (content-convergent). Re-validated (2026-09-12, #2868) — the `planning → implementation` tests-commit left the seeded suite files dirty in the root tree while the local `main`/`spec` refs lagged; the SI cleared the residue with a force checkout and then reset the local spec ref to the origin tip (G-032 form) before the tester-serving checkout, so the transition created `spec/2868` and the root served the true tip with no collision and no duplicate tests-commit. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2870) — the `planning → implementation` transition created `spec/2870` first-attempt via the worktree-independent ref op while the seeded suites sat dirty; each rework re-entry (`testing → implementation`, twice) re-ran the content-convergent tests-commit, and the SI cleared the residue with a force checkout + a G-032 ref reset before each tester-serving checkout. The root's dirty `.opencode/tests/**` residue was force-cleared each time (`git checkout -f -B spec/2870 origin/spec/2870`); no collision stalled a transition.

- **re-validated:** 2026-09-13, #2872 — the `planning → implementation` tests-commit left the local `main` ref lagging `origin/main` (4 commits) with the four seeded `.opencode/tests/desktop-chrome/*.md` files dirty in the root working tree, which blocked the tester-serving checkout (`git checkout -B spec/2872 origin/spec/2872` refused; the SI had to use `git checkout -f -B`, the G-107-family recovery). The transition itself succeeded (no collision, no duplicate tests-commit); the residue is the known G-107/G-122 recovery shape. Candidate durable fix: the `tests-commit` action should advance the local `main` ref (or leave the checkout clean) after pushing.

### G-123: capsule_omits_continuous_state_requirement
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-10
- **observed:** #2853 — the plan's ST-3 enumerated only the discrete interaction call-sites (click/joke, double-click/game, teleport, TicTacToe callbacks) and omitted the REQ-4 continuous "in use" suppression (an open TicTacToe / an active joke stream must hold the idle timer down), even though the QA Plan's REQ-4 edge and the Architect's own frozen Discussion bullet required it. The developer faithfully implemented the capsule, so a test that opened TicTacToe and left it idle saw the companion auto-return 21 s later with the game still open (round-1 FAIL). The declared requirement was never carried into a `- [ ]` line — a scheduled gap, not a code deviation.
- **target_failure:** a declared requirement whose behavior is a continuous state ("while X is in use", "throughout Y") is omitted from the decomposition because the capsule enumerates only the discrete transition call-sites, so implementation matches the capsule exactly and the requirement surfaces only at testing.
- **guardrail:** Every EARS `WHILE`/continuous clause MUST own an explicit `- [ ]` sub-task line (with its own non-goals), separate from the discrete transition call-sites; the SI's plan review maps each continuous clause to a checklist line before convergence. A requirement with no owning line is a plan defect returned to the Architect in the same pass — never left to the tester to surface.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (Decompose step) + playbooks/self-improver.md (plan-review continuous-clause mapping) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** **Confirmed** (2026-09-11, #2854) — the Architect decomposed every continuous clause (`thinking`/`joking` holds, the companion and desktop `playful` beats, desktop `thinking` while engaged/query) into its own checklist line, and the spec passed testing first-pass with no continuous-requirement gap; the plan-review continuous-clause mapping held. Re-validated (2026-09-11, #2855) — the plan decomposed its two continuous `WHILE` clauses (a readiness check in flight → wizard-only, never the normal controls; an install in flight → action disabled/running, no re-entrant install) into their own checklist lines; no continuous-requirement gap surfaced through implementation/testing (the single round-1 FAIL was an unrelated external winget identifier defect). Re-validated (2026-09-12, #2856) — the plan's continuous "never `present` while in flight / never complete while any file is absent" invariants owned explicit checklist lines; live sampling across the real ~3.7 GB pull never observed a false present/complete, and a partial set read incomplete and named the missing file. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2871) — the three continuous clauses (busy held for the whole stream; filter/launch reachable while active; single in-flight) each owned an explicit `- [ ]` line (ST-3/ST-2/ST-1); the round-2 tester confirmed the busy state held for the whole stream and Enter was a no-op.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-13, #2872 — the plan's three continuous `WHILE` clauses (the clock keeps advancing; the cluster is legible/identical in light AND dark; the covered-by-window z-sink holds while a maximized window is up) each owned an explicit checklist line on the single sub-task; the live round confirmed the clock advanced on its 60s timer and both themes rendered, with the z-sink intact. No continuous-requirement gap surfaced.

- **re-validated:** 2026-09-12, #2865 — the wizard's continuous clauses (WHILE a step is working, never a static screen; WHILE the first probe is checking, the row stays legible) each owned an explicit checklist line (R-2.1/R-2.5 → ST-2); the live round observed active indicators + changing narration in every working state. No continuous-requirement gap surfaced.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-12, #2868 — the two continuous clauses ("WHILE a Settings window is open, re-invoking focuses the existing window and never duplicates"; "WHILE the companion is not ready or the first probe is in flight, the Companion section renders only the setup wizard") each owned a dedicated capsule with an executable assertion (window dedup/focus/restore contract test; companion-gate shell test), and the live round observed both behaviors. No continuous-requirement gap surfaced.

- **re-validated:** 2026-09-15, #2877 — the plan's continuous `WHILE` clauses (the visible active-capture indicator held for the whole session; no idle engine/microphone while not listening; opt-in default) each owned explicit checklist lines (ST-5/ST-6/ST-9). The plan-level mapping held — the round-1 F-38 defect was a fix break in the owned line (a duplicate start cleared the indicator), not an unowned continuous requirement, and the rework restored the invariant live. No continuous-requirement gap surfaced.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-17, #2882 — the continuous "WHILE Space is held, listening continues and the indicator shows for the whole capture" requirement owned its own checklist line (ST-5, explicitly marked G-123), separated from the discrete keydown/keyup call-sites; the tester's real held-key legs (30,226 ms / 9,610 ms / 8,967 ms holds, 0 ms tap) confirmed the state held for the whole gesture and released cleanly. No continuous-requirement gap surfaced.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-19, #2897 — the plan's two continuous `WHILE` clauses (the distinct listening/processing/stopped/error states across a model-audio session; loopback-only delivery with no egress) each owned an explicit checklist line (ST-4 and ST-8, both marked G-123), separate from the discrete start/stop call-sites. The single round-1 FAIL was a break INSIDE an owned line (the `stopped` state had no turn-completion writer), not an unowned continuous requirement, and the rework restored it live in one round. No continuous-requirement gap surfaced.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-21, #2924 — the plan's three continuous `WHILE` clauses (maximized/full-bleed rect + radius/shadow + no grips; floating surface + 8 grips + min floors + ≥24px on-screen; every header = one tile + one title + zero monogram + labelled controls) each owned an explicit checklist line bound to ST-2/ST-3, separate from the discrete open/restore call-sites; the live round verified all three states. No continuous-requirement gap surfaced.

### G-124: per_webview_transient_state_ownership_unstated
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-10
- **observed:** #2853 — the companion's auto-hide flag was instantiated per-webview while the launcher mascot lives only in the main window. A per-window transient could not enforce the global "exactly one Fredo" invariant across the main and terminal windows: the non-host timer could fire first (two Fredos) or the host's could (zero Fredos), and a non-host window could fire while the user interacted in the host. Caught at triage only after an explicit ancestor/state-ownership trace; the plan then adopted a host-owned timer plus a global presence broadcast before implementation.
- **target_failure:** a feature whose transient state is instantiated per-webview (per Tauri window) while a related surface lives in another webview is planned with a local-only state model, so a global invariant (one entity at a time, a coordinated lifecycle) is unenforceable and surfaces late as a race or a duplicate.
- **guardrail:** For any feature with per-webview state and a cross-window surface, the Domain Model MUST include an explicit state-ownership + cross-window sync trace (which webview owns each transient value, which surface reads it, every existing cross-window channel), and the plan MUST name its coordination model (host-owned owner + global broadcast, or equivalent) and prove the global invariant across windows, not merely within one.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (Domain Model step) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** **Confirmed** (2026-09-11, #2854) — the plan's Domain Model included an explicit cross-surface state-ownership trace (the companion and the launcher mascot each own their local transient expression; no extension of the presence reducer or cross-window payload) and no cross-window race or duplicate-Fredo surfaced; the trace kept the slice's state model correct through implementation and testing first-pass. Re-validated (2026-09-11, #2855) — the plan's Domain Model included an explicit state-ownership trace (Companion readiness is mount-scoped React state in the main webview derived from the backend; the terminal window never mounts the settings panel; the backend is the only durable source, and the dev adapter's undefined `invoke` fails closed to not-ready) and no cross-window race or stale-cache issue surfaced.

### G-110: agent_self_provisioned_tools
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-07
- **observed:** #2835 — an agent needing a pre-fix baseline run (the buggy code sits on `main`, which the dev-environment tool refuses to serve — G-052) responded by hand-rolling its own runner: `.opencode/tmp/2835/start-main.mjs` spawned `cmd.exe /c pnpm dev:tauri` DETACHED via `bun`/node to serve the repo root on `main`, re-implementing `dev-env.ps1 -Action Up`'s internal Start-Process machinery in a scratch script.
- **target_failure:** an agent that hits a sandbox/allowlist/tooling wall (a denied verb, a fail-closed tool, a missing capability) responds by writing its OWN ad-hoc helper script that re-implements a sanctioned tool's internals (a detached dev-server spawn, a query runner, a port killer) — producing unmanaged processes, logs outside the sanctioned tooling, duplicated maintenance, and a second (unreviewed) implementation of infrastructure the pipeline already owns. The human's principle: **we provide the tools for our agents; agents never create their own tools.**
- **guardrail:** An agent that needs a capability the provisioned tooling lacks MUST block/escalate to the Self-Improver with the exact gap (the command it tried + why it was denied + what the provisioned tool lacks) rather than write a scratch script that re-implements a sanctioned tool's internals. Scratch `.opencode/tmp/<issue>/` is for DATA (drafts, dumps, evidence) — never for re-inventing infrastructure. Baseline/BEFORE measurements in research-first specs run against the pre-fix `spec/<N>` state through `dev-env.ps1 -Action Up -Spec <N>` (or `-At <pre-fix-sha>` for an ancestor of the spec tip); agents NEVER check out `main` for a baseline and NEVER hand-roll a detached `pnpm dev:tauri` spawn. A genuine cross-branch measurement need is a tooling request to the SI (a new script/param), not an ad-hoc agent script. The SI treats any agent-authored scratch runner under `.opencode/tmp/<issue>/` that starts a dev server / re-implements `dev-env.ps1` / `process-hygiene.ps1` / the query tools as a G-110 violation: kill the spawned processes, delete the artifact, document the gap, and (if the capability is genuinely missing) extend the sanctioned tool in the same pass.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (route subagent tool gaps) + skills/dev-environment/SKILL.md (baseline-leg rule) + dev-env.ps1 (`-At` baseline-leg param) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

- **re-validated:** 2026-09-12, #2865 — the pre-implementation BEFORE baseline was captured through the sanctioned `dev-env.ps1 -Action Up -Spec <N>` path at the pre-fix tip; no agent hand-rolled a runner or checked out `main`, and the UI/UX Expert read the resulting image files with its Read tool (its sandbox has no dev/webview tools). No G-110 violation.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-13, #2872 — the BEFORE rendered-geometry baseline for the spacing change ran through the sanctioned `dev-env.ps1 -Action Up -Spec 2872 -At d71fe7d` baseline leg (pre-fix ancestor of the `spec/2872` tip), then a standard `Up` restored the tip; no agent checked out `main`, and no scratch runner was written. The `-At` baseline leg is the sanctioned tool for exactly this case; no G-110 violation.

### G-111: keep_win_claim_asserted_without_tip_verification
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-08
- **observed:** #2835 — the SI's PO-facing escalation and PO-decision Status both asserted "session list not gated on the full Chat drain" as an already-proven keep-win. The Architect's code trace then proved it was NOT implemented at the round-2 tip: the session drawer was parked empty until the FULL Chat replay drain settle, and the round-2 fix plan had DELIBERATELY pinned that parked contract as the intended drawer gate. The claim originated in a developer Status self-description, was carried unverified into PO-facing records, and the PO repeated it as a premise ("keep the already-proven wins: delta replay, replay-window fix, list-not-gated-on-full-drain"). Only the Architect's file:line trace caught the false keep-win, and a whole additional fix round (ST-9-R3) was required to make the claim true.
- **target_failure:** an SI escalation/Status asserts a code behavior is "proven", "already won", or "kept" based on a developer's self-report or a prior round's Status, without tip-verification — so the PO decides on a false premise and the real defect (the very behavior claimed as kept) ships or requires a late fix round.
- **guardrail:** The SI MUST NOT assert a code behavior as "proven / already won / kept" in a Status, escalation, or audit framing unless it is verified at the CURRENT branch tip — by the tester's measured evidence or a Software-Architect code trace (file:line). A developer Status self-description is a claim, not evidence. When a "keep-win" claim will shape a PO decision, dispatch the Architect to verify it at the tip first, or phrase it as "developer-reported, not yet verified." The same rule applies when the SI reviews a developer push that claims an invariant: an assertion that a gate/behavior is NOT present is only trustworthy after a tip-level trace confirms the absence.
- **home:** references.md (this record) + playbooks/self-improver.md (Status/escalation drafting — "claims are not evidence")
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-112: fix_plan_pin_enshrines_the_user_visible_defect
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-08
- **observed:** #2835 — the round-2 Fix Plan's regression pin (ST-7-R2(a)) asserted the session drawer unlocks ONLY on the full Chat replay settle "and NEVER on row presence", codifying as an intended contract the exact empty-first-render behavior the PO's qualitative acceptance criterion ("Mission Monitor renders its sessions promptly — no ~seconds delay the first time") names as the defect. The pin made the bug a pinned invariant, so the subsequent PO direction (empty-first-render is the primary defect) forced re-authoring that same pin (ST-9-R3c) plus a product-code change to satisfy the re-authored contract.
- **target_failure:** a fix-plan regression pin codifies a user-visible behavior (a false-empty state, a blank-then-populate delay, a multi-second gate) as "intended" while it contradicts a qualitative acceptance criterion — so the pin actively blocks the fix the AC requires and the PO must later override it.
- **guardrail:** When a Fix Plan or regression pin freezes a behavioral gate/contract, the author MUST check it against the plan's QUALITATIVE acceptance criteria, not only against the round's internal invariants. A pin that enshrines a user-visible failure symptom (false-empty, delayed render, parked list) as intended is a defect, not a contract — phrase the pin around the AC's semantics (prompt render, list shows DB sessions, no blank-then-populate) and re-author any pin that contradicts them. The SI's fix-plan review checks pins against the qualitative ACs before approving the round.
- **home:** references.md (this record) + playbooks/software-architect.md (Fix Plan authoring — pins must satisfy the qualitative ACs) + playbooks/self-improver.md (fix-plan review)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-113: perf_budget_anchored_to_pipeline_polluted_corpus
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-08
- **observed:** #2835 — the round-1/2 perf budgets (cold first-open ≤ 1,000 ms, settle ≤ 1,500 ms, wire ≤ 40 batches) were anchored to the live `fredo.db` corpus: ~14,011 chat / ~17,431 tool rows. The PO later ruled that corpus was **test pollution** (repeated agentic-pipeline runs of mock opencode sessions), NOT a realistic user corpus, and superseded the whole budget framing — a full escalation gate (the SI's A/B/C hold) plus a PO reframe burned rounds measuring and defending budgets on the wrong surface, when the reachable budgets on a clean/fresh corpus were the actual acceptance surface all along.
- **target_failure:** a research-first perf spec anchors its numeric budgets (and its pass/fail gates) to an existing DB corpus whose composition is unvalidated — an accumulation of the agentic pipeline's own mock/test sessions passes for "realistic user data", so the budgets are either foregone-FAIL (cold open over a pollution-sized drain) or foregone-PASS on the wrong surface.
- **guardrail:** When a perf/regression spec's budgets are anchored to an existing corpus (live DB, accumulated history), the planning pass MUST validate the corpus's composition BEFORE anchoring numbers: provenance (are the sessions real user sessions or accumulated agentic-pipeline / mock test runs?), distinct-session count, row source mix, and recency-vs-retention shape. If the corpus is pipeline/test pollution, either clean it and budget against a fresh realistic corpus (a handful of real sessions), or explicitly scope the polluted corpus as a non-budget STRESS leg only. Never gate AC pass/fail on numbers measured against an unvalidated corpus; state the corpus provenance in the plan and the budget derivation basis.
- **home:** references.md (this record) + playbooks/qa-expert.md (budget derivation — corpus provenance before numbers) + playbooks/software-architect.md (perf-spec Domain Model cites corpus composition)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-108: pure_rendering_feature_blocked_by_telemetry_only_live_gate
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-06
- **observed:** #2824 round 2
- **target_failure:** a `testing → audit` transition is refused for a presentational-only feature (no telemetry/emission surface) because the live-evidence gate token-matches only the telemetry-spans table, while the documented policy (tester + qa-expert playbooks) accepts live evidence as "telemetry OR DOM/screenshot receipts" for a live-verified UI feature.
- **guardrail:** Hardened the testing-exit live-evidence gate: a live-rendered webview receipt now also satisfies live evidence — recognized by an `upload-evidence` raw URL under the evidence directory on the spec branch, a live webview tool receipt (DOM snapshot / screenshot), or a live rendered-geometry measurement (`getBoundingClientRect`). These are receipts only a tester who actually drove the running webview can produce, so a bare screenshot filename or local scratch path still does NOT count (it is already refused by the unviewable-evidence guard). Validated: test-scripts.ps1 97/97.
- **home:** references.md (G-108)
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-11, #2854 — an eighth pure-rendering UI spec (shared avatar status vocabulary; live policy) passed `testing` with rendered-webview receipts (per-state DOM/animation fingerprints, screenshots, measured geometry) plus a `telemetry_spans` live-query receipt; the `testing → audit` gate accepted the round-1 PASS and auto-merged PR #2860. No false "static-only PASS" rejection.
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-06, #2824 round 2) — the gate refused the transition for the pure-rendering launcher keycap spec; after the hardening the same PASS verdict (rendered-webview evidence) cleared the gate and the spec merged. Re-validated (2026-09-06, #2830 round 1) — a fourth pure-rendering desktop-chrome consolidation spec (single top-right status LED, no telemetry surface) passed `testing` with rendered-webview receipts (upload-evidence raw URLs under `.opencode/evidence/2830/` + DOM snapshots + a getBoundingClientRect 12px/16px measurement) and the `testing → audit` gate accepted the PASS and merged the PR. ### G-109: developer_silently_drops_ac_named_observable
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-06
- **observed:** #2827 round 1 — the developer silently DROPPED the mouth from the PixelButler base form, asserting "the guide has no separate mouth (just the chin/neck arc)" and citing the QA Expert's conditional phrasing while ignoring the ACs' explicit "specific eyes/**mouth**/body base form" (AC-1/AC-2/AC-3) and the issue body. The plan ST-1 explicitly said "ADD the small horizontal mouth". The SI caught it at the step-7 review, verified against the authoritative reference guide (whose NEUTRAL/base expression shows a small horizontal mouth) + the ACs, and returned the work; the dev then added the mouth and the vision gate passed.
- **target_failure:** a developer overrides an explicit AC-named observable based on a subjective contrary reading of a reference asset (a design guide / wireframe / image), silently dropping or adding scope, and the deviation only surfaces at audit/test — or worse, ships.
- **guardrail:** An AC-named observable is BINDING at implementation. A developer must NOT silently drop or add an explicit AC feature based on a subjective reading of a reference asset that contradicts the AC prose; where a reference-asset reading conflicts with the AC, the developer flags the conflict to the SI (who reconciles against the authoritative reference + the AC, G-023-style) rather than self-serving the AC. The SI's step-7 review (review each dev push against the plan's scope + ACs) is the enforcement point: return failed work with a focused change list BEFORE the tester round, not at audit.
- **home:** references.md (this record) + playbooks/self-improver.md (step-7 review: "Review each dev's pushes on spec/N against the plan's scope + acceptance criteria... return failed work to the same developer with a focused change list")
- **effectiveness:** **Partial** (2026-09-17, #2882) — a QA-REQ-named observable (the `release Space to finish` chip plus the Enter-during-a-live-capture no-op) was absent from the implementation and the developer's own completion report did not flag the omission; it was caught by the merged-tip named-observable grep (G-162), not by the developer's self-report, and a focused in-flight fix landed it before testing. The enforcement point held at the orchestrator's review, but the self-report gap persists.






### G-107: spec_branch_sync_sandbox_gaps_and_utf8_corruption
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-05
- **observed:** #2823 — syncing `spec/2823` with an advancing `origin/main`: the `planning → implementation` transition runs `tests-commit` (suite → main) BEFORE creating `spec/<N>`, so the branch forks stale (its `functional.md` ended at F-15 while `main` had the #2823 F-16..F-20 cases). The G-032 merge stalled for two sandbox reasons: (a) `git checkout -- <path>` and `git restore` are BOTH denied (the `git checkout*` allow covers only the `-B` branch-reset form, not the `--` path-restore form), so a dirty/deleted tracked file cannot be discarded via git; (b) `Remove-Item` left them as unstaged deletions, which still blocks the merge. Recovery — the reset idiom: `Remove-Item` the dirty tracked files → `git checkout -B spec/<N> <sha>` (resets branch + clean worktree) → `git merge <origin/main SHA>` → `git push origin HEAD:spec/<N>`. Separately, materializing a git blob into the worktree via `git show <ref>:<path> > <path>` SILENTLY wrote **UTF-16** (PowerShell 5.1 `>` default), corrupting the UTF-8 suite files (git then treats them as binary and reports a misleading `0 insertions, 0 deletions` stat); the corrupt commit was discarded via the same reset.
- **target_failure:** a spec-branch/G-032 sync stalls because the sandbox denies `git checkout --`/`git restore`, OR a UTF-8 file is silently corrupted by using PowerShell `>` to materialize a git blob.
- **guardrail:** NEVER use `git show <ref>:<path> > <path>` to materialize a blob into the worktree — PowerShell `>` writes UTF-16 and corrupts UTF-8 text; use the `write` tool or `Set-Content -Encoding utf8`. For a stale/in-flight spec-branch sync, use the reset idiom — `Remove-Item <dirty tracked files>` → `git checkout -B <branch> <sha>` (clean worktree) → `git merge <origin/main SHA>` → `git push origin HEAD:spec/<N>` — and treat `git checkout --`/`git restore` denial as an `opencode.json` allowlist gap to propose to the human (not a workaround to fight).
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (step 9 G-032/G-038 sync — extending G-026's sandbox-merge workaround) + references.md (G-107)
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-10, #2853 — the reset idiom ran at both testing handoffs (remove the dirty seeded suite paths, then the branch-reset checkout to the spec tip); the `git checkout -- <path>` denial recurred, and the working restore form was the tree-ish pathspec restore (the `git checkout <tree-ish> <path>` form, no `--`), which cleanly restored the deleted tracked files.
- **effectiveness:** **Confirmed** (2026-09-05, #2823) — the G-032 sync required the reset idiom after both the `checkout --` denial and the UTF-16 corruption; the branch reached the tester synced (F-16..F-20 present). Re-validated (2026-09-06, #2830) — the `git checkout -- <path>`/`git restore` denial recurred while discarding the QA-seeded suite edits carried into the spec working tree; the orchestrator used `git checkout -- .` (the non-path form) and the reset idiom (`git checkout -B spec/2830 origin/spec/2830` + main-tip SHA merge + push) to re-sync the branch, which reached the tester synced with main's current suite guidance. Re-validated (2026-09-10, #2852) — the `testing → audit` handoff needed the same reset idiom to sync `spec/2852` with `main`'s 8 tester-suite commits (remove the dirty tracked suite files → fast-forward local `main` to `origin/main` → merge the `origin/main` tip SHA → push `HEAD:spec/2852`); the PR then cleared its BLOCKED (behind-main) merge state and merged. The `planning → implementation` variant (worktree-coupled branch creation colliding with the planners' dirty seeded suites) is now hardened in the machine — see G-122. Re-validated (2026-09-11, #2854) — the delete-then-reset idiom ran cleanly at both the testing handoff and the doc-sync branch switch (remove the dirty QA-seeded suite paths, then `git checkout -B` to the target ref); the root reached `spec/2854` clean at the origin tip and later `main` at origin.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-21, #2922 — the G-032/G-038 sync ran at the rework handoff. The `git checkout --`/`git restore` denials recurred, and the documented `Remove-Item` + branch-reset form did NOT by itself restore the deleted tracked files (the reset preserved the local deletions). The working one-step form was the FORCE reset checkout — the `-f` variant of the branch-reset checkout to the remote spec tip — which restored the worktree and cleared the deletions in a single command, after which the main-tip SHA merge and the spec-head push completed cleanly. Prefer the `-f` branch-reset checkout over the two-step remove-then-reset when discarding already-persisted suite edits.

### G-106: draw_order_probe_skips_pointer_events_none
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-05
- **observed:** #2821 round 1 — the tester marked R4/T4.3 FAIL using `elementFromPoint` at the Online-LED centre, which returned the app drawer's status `<p>` — reading as "the two bottom status LEDs are occluded by the open app drawer." The actual draw-order was CORRECT (the LED wrapper is `position: fixed; z-index: 1210`, above the drawer's `z-index: 1200`), and the visual screenshot showed the LEDs un-occluded on top of the drawer. The failure was a measurement artifact: the `StreamStatus` LED wrapper is deliberately `pointer-events: none` (a passive, non-interactive status indicator by design), and CSSOM/WHATWG hit-testing SKIPS `pointer-events:none` elements, so `elementFromPoint`/`elementsFromPoint` cannot return the LED and falls through to whatever is genuinely underneath (here the open drawer). The round-2 corrected probe (pointer-events toggle + computed z-index + un-occluded screenshot) PASSED; no product code change was required and the round-1 FAIL burned a full retry round.
- **target_failure:** a draw-order / visual-overlap QA check uses `elementFromPoint`/`elementsFromPoint` on a passive (`pointer-events: none`) overlay indicator, producing a false "occluded/overlapped" FAIL (with a plausible-looking hit-test result) that costs a full retry round on a correct surface.
- **guardrail:** A draw-order/overlap probe targeting a passive indicator (`pointer-events: none`) MUST NOT use a bare `elementFromPoint`/`elementsFromPoint` — use one of: (a) a pointer-events toggle probe (set the indicator's `style.pointerEvents='auto'`, re-probe, assert the indicator is the topmost hit, restore the style), (b) a pixel/colour sample from a screenshot (assert the point is the indicator's state colour, not the surface beneath), or (c) a computed-z-index comparison within the same nearest stacking-context ancestor. QA/architect should state this in the plan's verification method for any spec with a passive overlay indicator; a "hit test returns the element beneath" result on a `pointer-events:none` indicator is a technique false-negative, not an occlusion defect.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (verification judgement — treat a hit-test fall-through on a `pointer-events:none` indicator as a technique artifact) + playbooks/qa-expert.md / playbooks/tester.md (draw-order probe method) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** **Confirmed** (2026-09-05, #2821 round 1 — the false FAIL was root-caused to this skip behavior; the round-2 corrected probe passed, no product change needed). Re-validated (2026-09-06, #2825 round 1) — the tester again faced two `pointer-events:none` overlays (LauncherChrome + StreamStatus LED pair) over DOM `IconButton` controls and used the G-106 methods (pointer-events toggle for the hit-target ACs, computed-z-index comparison for the stacking AC, pixel/screenshot for the logo-band AC) — no false occlusion FAIL, no round burned. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2872) — for the maximized-window cluster-vs-titlebar-controls AC the tester used method a on the passive chrome band (force the band root `pointerEvents='auto'`, `elementFromPoint` at each Min/Restore/Close centre returns the window control, restore) PLUS method c (band z 1200 uncovered → 0 covered); no bare `elementFromPoint`, no false occlusion FAIL.

### G-105: reference_image_discovery_glob_false_negative
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-05
- **observed:** #2821 (post-merge launcher-surface defect spec) — a recurring post-merge defect where agents locate reference images via `glob`, but the Glob tool EXCLUDES dot-directories (`.opencode/`, `.github/`, `.worktrees/`), so `.opencode/wireframes/*.png` and `.opencode/wireframes/bugs/*.png` report "no files found" even though the images exist on disk (confirmed present via a directory listing: all six tracked wireframes + the five `bugs/` captures are real PNGs, not LFS/Git-LFS pointers). This is the same root cause as G-024 (the dot-dir blind spot), but it was "noted" and never enforced as a discovery rule — so it recurred on the reference-image path independently of the pipeline-state preflight path (test suites, workflow files, tmp artifacts) that G-024 already guards.
- **target_failure:** a planner/developer/tester that must load a reference image (design wireframe, mockup, avatar/canvas guide, or a defect "current wrong-state" capture) concludes the asset "doesn't exist" from a glob "no files found", producing a false missing-asset diagnosis, a silently dropped visual-fidelity/acceptance gate, or a spurious FAIL on an image that is actually present under a dot-directory.
- **guardrail:** Reference-image / desktop-wireframe discovery MUST never use `glob` to prove (or disprove) existence. Agents load a reference image by `Read <explicit absolute path>` or confirm it via a directory listing (`Test-Path` / `Get-ChildItem`); a glob "no files found" only means "not indexed" (dot-directories are excluded by the Glob tool), never "does not exist". When an agent reports it "can't find" a referenced image, the SI treats that as a G-024/G-105 false negative (NOT a missing file) and requires it to Read/Test-Path the exact declared path first. To make this enforceable rather than merely noted: every dispatch brief (architect/developer/tester) that consumes reference images MUST list the explicit absolute paths and state the no-glob rule; and the SI's planning/convergence pass directory-lists every declared reference-image path in the plan and flags any planner that reports a referenced image as absent.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md Guardrails (line — reference-image discovery + dispatch-brief rule) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** **Confirmed** (2026-09-05, #2821) — verified at dispatch: the reference images were located via a directory listing after the Glob-style blind spot would otherwise have reported them absent; the explicit-path dispatch brief prevented any "image not found" false negative from reaching planning or the tester. Re-validated (2026-09-06, #2824): the reference wireframe (`esc-close`, `.opencode/wireframes/bugs/`) was Read by explicit absolute path in every phase (planners, tester) and the tester's round-2 verdict read it by explicit path; no "image not found" false negative reached planning or the audit. Re-validated (2026-09-06, #2825): all three planners + the developer + the tester consumed the two bug wireframes (`fredo-logo-ontop.png`, `windows-buttons-time-overlap.png`) via explicit-path Reads (never glob); the SI directory-verified both in the planning pass. Re-validated (2026-09-06, #2827): the reference images (`avatar-guide.png`, `avatar-current.png`) were Read by explicit absolute path throughout — all three planners, the developer's ST-3 vision self-check, the tester's live vision gate, and the SI's planning verification; no "image not found" false negative reached any phase, and the no-glob dispatch-brief rule held end-to-end. Re-validated (2026-09-13, #2872) — four reference images (`desktop-light`, `desktop-light-dark-theme-compare`, `bugs/windows-buttons-time-overlap`, `bugs/led-overlay`) were declared by explicit ABSOLUTE path in the Architect/UI/UX/tester briefs with the no-glob rule stated; every consumer Read them by explicit path (the vision pass reported reading all four), and no "image not found" false negative reached any phase.

### G-104: tests_runs_prose_image_ref_trips_renderability_guard
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-05
- **observed:** #2819 round 1 — the tester's `## Tests Runs` FAIL draft was refused at the `post-comments` flush by the evidence-renderability guard (#2756), because the guard flags ANY line containing `.png`/`.jpg`/`.jpeg` WITHOUT an `https://` on the SAME line. The offending lines were the tester's PROSE references to the authoritative wireframe asset (`` `desktop-light.png` `` in a "serving checkout / caveats" line that carried no `https://` URL) — a legitimate asset name, NOT an unviewable evidence screenshot. Workaround: drop the `.png` extension in prose lines that carry no `https://` (write "the `desktop-light` wireframe"); keep the `https://` raw URL in every Screenshot-cell image reference.
- **target_failure:** a tester verdict draft is refused at flush because a prose mention of a reference asset (wireframe/compare image) by `.png` filename trips the evidence-renderability guard, stalling the loop with a false "unviewable evidence" block.
- **guardrail:** The `## Tests Runs` evidence-renderability guard (#2756) is a per-LINE heuristic: a line with `.png`/`.jpg`/`.jpeg` and NO `https://` is refused. When it can't distinguish a prose asset-name mention from a dead image link, the tester must (a) never write a reference asset's `.png` filename in a prose line that carries no accompanying `https://` (name it without the extension), and (b) keep every ACTUAL evidence screenshot in a Screenshot cell as an `upload-evidence` `https://` raw URL. The SI should not delete the draft and re-write it silently — fix the prose mention so the verdict posts through its intended channel (the transition / manual `post-comments` flush).
- **home:** playbooks/tester.md (verdict draft — evidence-renderability guard) + playbooks/self-improver.md (draft-flush discipline G-078) + references.md (G-104)
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-17, #2886 — both verdict drafts (the round-1 FAIL and the round-2 PASS) flushed clean through the machine; the SI's tester brief carried the guard up front (descriptive link labels, image extensions only on lines carrying an https URL), so no renderability-guard refusal occurred.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-20, #2914 — the SI's tester brief again carried the guard up front (evidence frames named without image extensions in prose; `.png`/`.jpeg` tokens only on lines carrying an `https://` URL; descriptive link labels). The `## Tests Runs (round 1)` draft flushed clean with seven `upload-evidence` user-attachment URLs and no renderability-guard refusal.
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-06, #2824 round 2) — re-fired at the `post-comments` flush for the same reason: the tester's `tests-runs.md` prose line mentioned the reference wireframe by `.png` (and the markdown Screenshot-cell LINK LABELS were bare filenames `[ac1-engaged-dark.png]`), so the guard refused on lines carrying `.png` without `https://` on the SAME line. Applied the documented fix: dropped the `.png` extension in prose, and made the link LABELS descriptive (the `https://` URL stays in the target, only the label changed). The verdict then posted through its intended channel. Prevented (2026-09-06, #2825 round 1) — the SI's tester brief carried the G-104 guard up front (name wireframes without `.png` in prose, keep `.png` tokens only on lines with an `https://` URL, descriptive link labels), so the `## Tests Runs (round 1)` draft flushed clean with no renderability-guard refusal. Re-validated (2026-09-11, #2855) — both the round-1 FAIL and round-2 PASS verdict drafts flushed clean; the SI's tester brief carried the guard up front (descriptive link labels, `.png` tokens only on lines carrying an `https://` URL). Re-validated (2026-09-12, #2856) — both the round-1 FAIL and the round-2 PASS verdict drafts flushed clean; the SI's tester brief again carried the guard up front. **Re-fired (2026-09-13, #2872)** — the guard refused the vision-verdict attribution line because it named the local evidence frame paths (`.jpeg` with no `https://` on the SAME line); the tester fixed it by describing the frames in prose. Root cause was SI-side: the #2872 tester brief did NOT carry the G-104 guard up front (unlike #2825/#2855/#2856), so this guard's prevention depends on the SI actually inlining it in every tester brief — the brief template must state it (name evidence frames without image extensions in prose; `.png`/`.jpeg` tokens only on lines carrying an `https://` URL; descriptive link labels). **Re-fired (2026-09-20, #2905)** — third refusal, same shape: the tester's prose named bare local screenshot filenames (`.png`) with no `https://` on the SAME line while the Screenshot cells carried raw URLs; remedy applied at the flush (name the frame without the extension / inline the raw URL). Root cause was SI-side again — the brief had omitted the guard (as in #2872). Durable fix landed this pass: the guard is now INLINED in the self-improver playbook's tester-dispatch step, so every tester brief carries it.

### G-103: removal_slice_reference_set_under_enumerated
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-05
- **observed:** #2810 round 1 — the PO backlog body enumerated `@maomaolabs/core` as owned only by `apps/ui` (`package.json:21`, `main.tsx:5`, the `fredo-desktop.css` shim, stale comments). The architect's research found the dependency ALSO in `apps/tauri` (`package.json:16`, `main.tsx:2` CSS import, the `@fredo/ui/styles` consumer import, the `vite.config.ts` alias, plus lockfile importers for both workspaces). The ACs' `apps/ui/` grep was satisfiable narrowly, but deleting the shim without the `apps/tauri` consumers broke the workspace build, so the plan had to extend deletion-only co-changes into `apps/tauri` and escalate the scope discovery to the human.
- **target_failure:** a dependency/refactor removal slice's issue body under-enumerates the affected files (only the named package), forcing a mid-run scope discovery and in-slice co-changes the PO did not enumerate.
- **guardrail:** For any dependency-removal or reference-purge slice, the Architect MUST grep the ENTIRE workspace for the target symbol (package name / import path / class selector / exported symbol — including a deleted export from a shared package such as `@fredo/ui` that a SIBLING workspace imports), not just the named package, so the reference set is complete before ACs are finalized. If the reference set spans a second workspace or a consumer export/alias graph, either include the co-changes in-slice (deletion-only, no re-authoring) or make the AC's grep scope explicit and note the surviving references for the human; never let a first-pass plan discover the residual surface only at merge/test time.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (Domain Model — cross-package symbol grep) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Partial (re-validated 2026-09-05, #2817 — the removal slice under-enumerated a DELETED-EXPORT consumer: the Architect verified the bare deps (`ogl`/`postprocessing`/`gsap`) whole-repo, but the animation-surface reference set listed only `apps/ui`, while `apps/tauri/src/main.tsx` also imported `AnimationProvider` from `@fredo/ui`; the developer caught it mid-implementation via the build (not at merge/test) and added the necessary in-slice consumer removal, so the guardrail's cross-package grep was only PARTIALLY applied — the deleted-export-across-a-sibling-workspace consumer was not in the plan's reference set.)

### G-102: measurement_acs_blocked_by_sandbox_denied_actions_verbs
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-03
- **observed:** #2801 round 1 — the two timing-measurement ACs (AC1 median PR wall-clock, AC4 warm-cache re-run) of a config-only CI spec were UNVERIFIED because the sandbox denies `gh api` (Actions REST per-job `started_at`/`completed_at`, step-log cache-hit lines) and `gh run rerun` (warm re-run trigger) to the tester AND the SI. The QA Plan spelled test cases whose evidence depends on exactly these denied verbs, so the ACs could never be evidenced in-sandbox.
- **target_failure:** a CI/measurement AC's verification depends on GitHub Actions REST or re-run verbs the sandbox denies, producing an unverifiable AC that is mis-classified as a product defect or is silently asserted clean.
- **guardrail:** (1) When authoring a QA Plan for a config/CI/performance spec whose ACs measure wall-clock/cache timing, specify the evidence path against the ALLOWED tool surface (a `gh pr checks` job-duration projection) and state up front which timing verbs (`gh api`, `gh run rerun`) are out-of-reach — do NOT write test cases whose verdict depends on a denied verb. (2) If a measurement AC cannot be evidenced in-sandbox, keep the row UNVERIFIED-with-named-blocker (G-053) and route it to the PO as a documented-partial decision; an honest PASS is substantiated ONLY when the PO explicitly accepts the partial (G-033 carve-out: a PO-accepted partial row is not FAIL) — never assert a measured speedup the available run contradicts. (3) A single cold full-matrix run may be SLOWER than baseline (new cache-restore + path-filter overhead, cold cache); record the honest aggregate and do not claim the AC's "faster" outcome from it — the real lever is the verified path-skip mechanism, not a measured median.
- **home:** playbooks/tester.md (Test case authoring — evidence-path matches the allowed tool surface) + playbooks/self-improver.md (loop review — PO-accepted-partial verdict discipline) + references.md (G-102)
- **effectiveness:** Pending (re-validated 2026-09-05, #2810 — applied cleanly for a static-policy deletion slice whose AC5 named `cargo check`, not in the tester's sandbox allowlist: the tester substituted an allowed build command (`pnpm --filter @fredo/tauri build:webview`), reasoned no Rust source changed, and explicitly noted the CI `rust-validate` covers the Rust gate — NO fake-verify, NO defect mis-classification, evidence-path matched the allowed surface. Re-validated 2026-09-03, #2803 (admin-verb sibling case, same discipline). <2 specs since activation, so Pending per the effectiveness recipe.)

### G-092: on_the_go_improvement
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-02
- **observed:** #2788 round 6
- **target_failure:** (on-the-go pipeline improvement)
- **guardrail:** Tool-discipline guardrail (4th stall this spec): the round-6 tester stalled in an unbounded webview-polling loop (malformed arg re-sent dozens of times, never sanity-checked) polling for a UI condition instead of the bounded wait tool — the human stopped the round. The 4-rule tool-discipline block is durable text: (1) poll a running webview only with the timeout-bounded wait tool, never a loop of repeated evaluate calls; (2) never repeat an identical tool call more than 3 times — after 3 unexpected results, record expected-vs-actual as FAIL/UNVERIFIED-with-named-blocker and move to the next leg (an honest FAIL advances the pipeline, a stall does not); (3) sanity-check tool arguments before sending (a malformed argument is a parameter mix-up, not a transient failure); (4) time-box the round — post the verdict with completed legs plus explicit FAIL/UNVERIFIED lines for incomplete ones. Retained guidance that worked this spec: pre-brief environment failures as surface-dont-spelunk with the sanctioned purge named, and pin codebase exemplar file:lines for every copy-existing-pattern decision.
- **home:** docs/agentic-pipeline/playbooks/tester.md (Tool discipline — bounded polling only) + docs/agentic-pipeline/playbooks/developer.md (Tool discipline — bounded retries)
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-10, #2853 — both tester rounds stayed within the discipline: bounded `tauri_webview_wait_for` polling, a page-level interval recorder instead of an unbounded async-sampling loop, and an honest `Wait failed` note treated as cosmetic rather than retried indefinitely; no stall, each round posted a verdict.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-11, #2855 — both tester rounds stayed within the discipline (bounded waits, no repeated identical calls, time-boxed verdicts); round 1 posted an honest FAIL with a traced root cause and round 2 a fresh in-session PASS — no stall across a two-round spec.
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-13, #2872 — the round stayed time-boxed and posted a verdict (no unbounded polling); its one tool-discipline miss was a synchronous wall-clock busy-wait implemented in webview JS, which wedged the webview — now guarded separately as G-152 (never implement a wall-clock wait in webview JS; use the timeout-bounded wait tool or a sanctioned bounded-sleep script).
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-02, #2788 round 6 re-dispatch) — the fresh tester session held all four rules (3 bounded waits max, no repeated identical calls, one honest named-blocker sub-leg instead of a loop) and completed the round with a verdict in a single session; the pre-dispatch block also contained a mandated stop condition. One in-spec application; watch on the next testing-heavy spec.



### G-091: on_the_go_improvement
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-02
- **observed:** #2788 round 5
- **target_failure:** (on-the-go pipeline improvement)
- **guardrail:** Stale CI-claim fix: the playbook said GitHub CI was deactivated (merge guard on merge-state alone) but validate.yml is LIVE (repo public) and rust-validate runs cargo check/test --locked + clippy -D warnings — a 4-lint clippy failure reached merge-time on #2788 round 5 because no dev receipt ever ran clippy. Playbook now requires the full local CI-parity set (CONTRIBUTING.md flags) in dev receipts. Harness untouched.
- **home:** references.md (G-091)

Re-validated (2026-09-11, #2855) — both developer dispatches ran the full local CI-parity set (`cargo check`/`test`/`clippy -D warnings` + UI build/tests) and reported exact output; `rust-validate` passed at the merge gate first-try after both the round-1 push and the round-2 fix. The tester sandbox lacks `cargo`, so CI remained the Rust gate — consistent with the local-parity rule (the developer ran it).

### G-090: on_the_go_improvement
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-02
- **observed:** #2788 round 5
- **target_failure:** (on-the-go pipeline improvement)
- **guardrail:** Machine fix (state machine, SI domain). The Development Summary round stamp was one low on every rework round — the draft flushes at the implementation to testing transition BEFORE the destination testing phase.started append, so the stamp named the round that just ended instead of the round whose work the summary documents (observed on #2788 where the round 3, 4 and 5 summaries posted as rounds 2, 3 and 4). Development Summary now stamps the testing-entry count plus one (a fresh issue still stamps round 1) while Tests Runs and SI Summary keep the raw count because they document the round that is ending. Documented in the same pass at the stamp site and in retry_state. New harness test covers the fresh-issue and rework edges. Full harness green (95 passed).
- **home:** references.md (G-090)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-02, #2788 round 6) — the round-6 Development Summary and Tests Runs both machine-stamped round 6 correctly on the rework round (previously every retry round stamped one low).






### G-089: on_the_go_improvement
- **activation_date:** 2026-08-31
- **observed:** #2773 round 1
- **target_failure:** (on-the-go pipeline improvement)
- **guardrail:** Docs/config-only specs must NOT gate verification on native builds: the Rust toolchain builds the vendored native inference dependency through CMake/Visual Studio discovery, which the developer sandbox's restricted shell cannot run, so a docs-only spec burned a round on build-environment diagnostics instead of spec work. Verification scope must match the diff surface - build hygiene applies to code changes only, and native-build legs belong only in plans whose diff touches code. Related lessons from the same run: settle git configuration questions with the origin-annotated config query command, never by reading config files (the effective value can come from a config origin invisible to file reads), and never glob, probe, or diagnose outside the repository in the sandbox.
- **home:** references.md (G-089)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-08-31, #2773 rounds 1-2) — recorded mid-run and applied immediately: the QA plan was amended to docs-only validations (native-build legs removed, tester briefed to never run them) and the spec completed with zero further build-environment stalls. Re-validated (2026-09-03, #2797): a GitHub-config-only spec kept verification `static` (no `cargo`/`pnpm` gate; offline harness + GitHub-REST assertions + product-tree-untouched check) and merged cleanly without a build-environment stall — G-089 held.
