- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-065: qa_comment_prefixes_with_no_consumer
- **activation_date:** 2026-08-25
- **observed:** #2756 full-timeline audit: across 16 rounds and 2,826 comments, exactly ONE `## Question` and ONE free-form `## Decision` were posted — while `## Tests Runs`/`## Fix Plan` carried 15 each. Workers never read comment threads by design (LEAN briefs inline what they need; workers read the context block + targeted artifacts), so a Q/A channel on the timeline had no reader except the orchestrator, who already sees everything through its record review. Even the spec's one scope escalation went out as a `Status`, not a Question.
- **target_failure:** maintaining comment conventions that have no consumer — playbook text teaching agents to post into channels nobody reads, and guard complexity defending gates those channels don't feed.
- **guardrail:** A communication convention must name its READER. The agent-facing comment surface is `Status` only (blockers/escalations/PO amendments); ambiguity is a `block` action (`--reason`, label + SLA) resolved by the orchestrator and returned inlined in the re-dispatch brief; decisions reach the record through the machine (`audit-record --reason`) or PO amendments via `Status`. Before adding an agent-facing artifact or prefix, identify who reads it and when — if the answer is "the orchestrator, eventually," route it through the orchestrator's existing mechanisms instead.
- **home:** pipeline-state.rs (`comment` whitelist = Status; Question/Decision refused with pointers) + github.md comment conventions + principles.md (open-question rule) + playbooks (developer/tester/product-owner/self-improver) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-084: grep_tool_stalls_on_installed_plugin_path
- **activation_date:** 2026-08-30
- **observed:** #2770 rounds 3-4: the Grep tool stalls indefinitely when pointed at the installed-plugin path `~\.config\opencode\plugins\fredo.js` — the `~` home-dir path is not usable by the Grep tool in this sandbox on Windows, so the agent waits forever (two tester rounds lost before the SI root-caused it). G-047's original wording ("grep the installed file for the fix's defining symbol") prescribed the wrong TOOL for that path.
- **target_failure:** an agent follows a playbook instruction to search a home-dir file with the Grep tool and stalls permanently, burning the round.
- **guardrail:** NEVER use the Grep tool (or any search tool) against `~\.config\opencode\plugins\fredo.js` or any `~` home-dir path. Verify plugin currency sandbox-safely: (1) `Get-FileHash` the installed file vs `apps/opencode-plugin/dist/index.js` — equal SHA256 proves byte-equality and currency; (2) `Select-String -LiteralPath` with BUNDLE-FORM anchors — bundling flattens source expressions (source `time.created` → bundle `createdAt`), so derive anchors from the built dist file, never from `src/*.ts`.
- **home:** dev-environment SKILL.md (plugin prerequisite block) + AGENTS.md SDD Pipeline Hygiene mirror (human-approved) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-085: webview2_corrupt_http_cache_white_screen
- **activation_date:** 2026-08-30
- **observed:** #2770 rounds 2-5: WebView2 served a corrupt cached HTTP response for the Vite dev URL — the webview painted raw HTTP headers as the page document (white screen; app HTML never rendered). The entry survived app restarts and recurred on 4+ consecutive cold boots; a manual `/?cb=<ts>` cache-busting navigation recovered it every time. Misattributing it to a stale binary or the spec wasted diagnosis time.
- **target_failure:** a dev webview renders a stale/corrupt cached document (white screen or raw headers) and the wedge is blamed on the app, the spec, or the dev binary instead of the WebView2 HTTP cache.
- **guardrail:** Recognize the signature (raw HTTP headers painted as the page document, survives restarts, Vite dev URL): recover with a `/?cb=<ts>` cache-bust navigation FIRST. The durable fix is baked into dev-env.ps1 — every COLD `Up` clears the WebView2 HTTP cache (Cache, Code Cache, GPUCache only; localStorage/IndexedDB/Cookies preserved) before launching the app; do not disable or work around it, and do not clear the whole EBWebView profile (that destroys app dev state).
- **home:** .opencode/scripts/dev-env.ps1 (`Clear-WebView2HttpCache` on cold Up) + dev-environment skill + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-086: css_var_alpha_append_invalid_in_declaration_contexts
- **activation_date:** 2026-08-30
- **observed:** #2770 round 4: the R-2 nested-accent stripe never painted — the stripe's soft-shadow component alpha-appended hex digits onto a CSS custom property (`var(--border-color)33`); var() substitution splices tokens without re-lexing, so the appended digits stay a separate token and the browser drops the WHOLE declaration at computed-value time (box-shadow computed `none`). The repo docs themselves sanctioned the pattern for tints, so the defect class was doc-prescribed; a sibling site (`${accent}28` borders) failed identically. 25 live sites existed across features.
- **target_failure:** CSS emission code alpha-appends onto a `var()`/custom-property reference, the declaration is silently dropped by the browser, and the visual defect survives source review and jsdom tests because only computed-value behavior reveals it.
- **guardrail:** NEVER alpha-append hex digits onto a `var()` reference or custom property in any declaration (box-shadow, border, background, outline) — var() substitution splices tokens without re-lexing, and the browser drops the entire declaration. Derive var()-based tints with the shared `tint()` helper (`color-mix(in srgb, … %, transparent)`); alpha-append is valid ONLY on literal hex strings (8-digit hex formed in JS before CSS parses). Any doc that sanctions a CSS emission pattern must be validated against browser computed values (e.g. `getComputedStyle` on a live run), not just source syntax — jsdom pins emission strings only.
- **home:** AGENTS.md Chakra section (human-approved rewrite) + apps/ui/src/shared/utils/colorTint.ts + chakra-ui-refactor SKILL.md + playbooks/ui-ux-expert.md + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-087: reopen_leg_reuses_squash_merged_evidence_branch
- **activation_date:** 2026-08-31
- **observed:** #2770 round 6 (the first reopen): the `done → planning` reopen reused the kept evidence branch `spec/2770`, whose pre-reopen commits had been SQUASH-merged to main — the histories diverge, so the round-6 PR was born CONFLICTING and the testing → audit merge was blocked until the developer ran the G-032 sync (merge origin/main into the spec branch, resolve to "final tree = main + fix-round delta", push).
- **target_failure:** a reopened feature's spec PR conflicts with main at merge time because the branch carries pre-reopen commits whose content main already absorbed via the squash — blocking the audit merge after testing already passed.
- **guardrail:** On a `done → planning` reopen, treat the spec branch as STALE BY CONSTRUCTION (it is the kept evidence branch; its pre-reopen work is already on main via squash): immediately after the `planning → implementation` transition, run the G-032 branch sync BEFORE any testing dispatch — merge origin/main into `spec/<N>`, resolve every conflict to the invariant "final tree = origin/main + the fix-round delta only", re-verify the build/tests, and push (fast-forward). Never force-push and never re-create the branch (it carries the evidence trail); the merge commit is the record.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (reopen workflow step) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-088: fixture_injected_evidence_closes_unmodeled_real_shapes
- **activation_date:** 2026-08-31
- **observed:** #2770 rounds 5-6: round 5's live drive validated depth-3 compact-card rendering entirely through FIXTURE-injected payloads (single cleanly-stamped composited copy per correlationId) — every depth-3 test passed while the real path failed, because real multi-hop re-key deliveries carry DOUBLE-stamped copies the fixtures never modeled. The reopen triage needed a real-corpus replay (the persisted real deliveries exported verbatim as a fixture) to reproduce and prove the fix.
- **target_failure:** a rendering/association behavior verified green by fixture-injected payloads ships broken because the real delivery path produces a shape the fixtures do not model (duplicate re-key copies, contested stamps, multi-hop compositing) — the mock-vs-real gap at fixture granularity.
- **guardrail:** A fixture may close a behavior class only when its injected payload shapes demonstrably model what the REAL delivery path emits for that class — for delegation depth ≥ 3, multi-hop re-keying, and composited-ownership behaviors this bar is NOT met by synthetic single-copy fixtures: require a live real-agent drive or a real-corpus replay (persisted deliveries exported verbatim and version-controlled) as the decisive evidence. When a defect survives a fixture-PASS round, replay the REAL persisted deliveries BEFORE redesigning any logic — the corpus reproduces the shape that fixtures hid.
- **home:** playbooks/tester.md (evidence-policy section) + playbooks/qa-expert.md (QA plan fixture boundary) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-093: qa_suite_overwritten_because_glob_claimed_absent
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-03
- **observed:** #2791 triage: the QA Expert seeded the `mission-monitor` durable suite because its `glob` check returned no files — concluding the feature domain "did not previously exist." But the suite DID pre-exist (the files show as tracked-modified at the `spec/2791` fork base); `glob` excludes `.opencode/`, so the "absent" conclusion was a G-024 false negative. The QA Expert then OVERWROTE the existing suite rather than extending it. Because `tests-commit` persists the suite to `main`, this can destroy prior cross-spec regression coverage (the suite is a cumulative shared asset) — caught by the SI's directory-listing cross-check in the orchestration snapshot, no round burned.
- **target_failure:** a planner (QA Expert) rewrites an existing durable feature suite from scratch because a glob-based "absent" check misled it, losing prior cross-spec regression cases that the suite had accumulated.
- **guardrail:** When seeding/extending a durable feature suite, detect the existing suite via a **directory listing/read** (never `glob`), and if the folder already has files, EXTEND them — never regenerate the suite from scratch. A glob "no files found" does NOT prove the suite is absent (G-024); a shared regression asset must grow, not be replaced. The SI's convergence pass must directory-check the declared `**Feature tests:**` folders and flag any planners that report a pre-existing suite as absent.
- **home:** playbooks/qa-expert.md (seed/extend step — detect via listing, extend not rewrite) + playbooks/self-improver.md (G-024 guardrail) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending


### G-094: escalating_verification_mode_adjustments
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-02
- **observed:** #2792 round 1: the tester FAILed solely because AR-3 (a failed tool call with NO captured error text → explicit absent-reason placeholder) was UNVERIFIED — its `success:false` + empty/absent-`error` observable could not be produced live (real opencode failures always emit a non-empty `tool.error`; the RTDB `toolError` merge rule is `LastNonZero`). The SI escalated a "PO scope decision required" `Status` and STOPPED the line. The human's proxy ruled this an **over-escalation**: it is an orchestration-level verification-mode decision (live↔static leg re-classification for an architecturally-unreachable defensive state) that changes NO requirement, NO acceptance criterion, and NO product code. The correct handling was QA case-work (the Software Architect's `scope` root-cause) + a PO amendment recorded as a Status comment + continue the line to done.
- **target_failure:** the SI treats a verification-mode gap (a test leg that cannot be executed live because the observable is architecturally unreachable) as a stop-the-line escalation, burning a human round and halting the pipeline for a decision that is the SI+QA's to make.
- **guardrail:** Verification-mode adjustments (live↔static leg re-classification) for unreachable/defensive states are handle by the SI + QA directly — record the amendment as a `Status` comment and continue the line. The SI's STOP triggers are ONLY: (1) scope changes, (2) changes to an AC's observable behavior, (3) product-code changes forced by new findings, (4) threats to a binding decision, (5) genuine BLOCKED guards. A test leg whose observable is architecturally unreachable does NOT stop the line — case-work it (QA root-cause + PO amendment) and proceed.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (escalation boundary discipline) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending


### G-095: rework_fixplan_or_stale_draft_spuriously_posts_on_success_audit
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-02
- **observed:** #2792: after the round-1 FAIL (AR-3 UNVERIFIED) the Software Architect authored `fix-plan.md` (root cause `scope`). The PO then resolved it with a verification amendment and NO rework. Two machine frictions surfaced: (1) `post_pending_comments` sets `is_rework_reentry = (from == "testing")`, which is TRUE for BOTH `testing → implementation` (rework) AND `testing → audit` (success) — a pending `fix-plan.md` at a `testing → audit` transition SPURIOUSLY posts as `## Fix Plan (round N)` even when the PO resolved with no rework; and (2) `update-plan --section qa` requires the assembled `triage-plan.md` draft, which is CONSUMED after the `planning → implementation` posting, so mid-`testing` the QA plan can only be updated via the A2A source (`triage.md`) or a reconstructed-then-removed draft (a reconstructed draft would be re-posted or auto-removed at the transition).
- **target_failure:** an obsolete/stale pending draft (a superseded fix plan, or a re-posted plan draft) leaks onto the timeline at a transition that should NOT post it — e.g. a `## Fix Plan (round N)` appearing on a PASS→audit that had no rework — corrupting the human's record and contradicting a no-rework PO resolution.
- **guardrail:** Before a `testing → audit` transition, neutralize any pending draft that should NOT post (a fix-plan superseded by a PO no-rework resolution: remove its `*Authored by*` footer and/or its `Root cause class:` line so the flush refuses it; observe the anti-spoofing/root-cause guards). Do NOT reconstruct the consumed `triage-plan.md` mid-phase just to satisfy `update-plan` — update the A2A source `triage.md` instead (the durable plan record) and record the amendment in the `Status` comment. Candidate machine-hardening: make `is_rework_reentry` distinguish `testing → audit` (success) from `testing → implementation` (rework), and allow `update-plan` to fall back to the A2A source when the assembled draft is consumed.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (draft-flush discipline) + pipeline-state.rs (candidate hardening)
- **effectiveness:** Pending


### G-096: telemetry_query_keyword_guard_substring_match
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-02
- **observed:** #2795 round 1 — the tester's live `telemetry_spans` cross-check queries were wrongly rejected by the read-only SQL keyword guard in the telemetry-query/wait-telemetry scripts because it matches forbidden keywords by SUBSTRING, not by token: any legitimate column whose name embeds a forbidden keyword (e.g. the `updated_at` column contains the substring `update`) was rejected as "forbidden keyword UPDATE" ("The `updated_at` column name contains the substring `update`, so any SELECT ... updated_at ... was rejected"). Worked around by querying the sanctioned `fredo.db` directly via `sqlite3 -readonly`.
- **target_failure:** the live-query guard rejects legitimate SQL because it flags forbidden keywords as substrings inside column/table names, forcing the tester off the sanctioned live-query path (and risking a "no evidence" false conclusion) precisely when a cross-check query needs a timestamp column.
- **guardrail:** The read-only query guard in the telemetry-query/wait-telemetry scripts MUST token-match SQL keywords (word-boundary aware), never substring-match, so a column like `updated_at`/`inserted_at` (which embeds `update`/`insert` as a substring) is not flagged. If a live-cross-check query is rejected, the tester should recognise it as a guard false-positive and run the identical query against the sanctioned DB (or fix the guard) rather than abandoning the live evidence path — the live `telemetry_spans` gate must not be defeated by a keyword-guard false positive.
- **home:** telemetry-query/wait-telemetry scripts (guard implementation) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Partial (re-validated 2026-09-12, #2865 — the tester's row-recency cross-check could not SELECT the `updated_at` column because the guard flagged the `update` substring inside the identifier; the tester worked around it via the spans table's `MAX(ingested_at)` + row counts. The false-positive class is still present; the guard should become identifier/word-boundary-aware. Prior: Pending from #2795.)


### G-097: github_rest_action_contract_not_verified_against_live_api
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-03
- **observed:** #2797 round 2 — three real GitHub-REST defects surfaced only by RUNNING the live hardening actions against the real repo, all from assuming the API contract from plan prose instead of the API: (1) the lock endpoint is `PUT .../issues/{n}/lock` (implementation used POST → 404 — a permissions failure would be 403, so the 404 was a method mismatch, mis-classified by the round-1 tester as `environment`); (2) the `lock_reason` enum value is `off-topic` (hyphenated), not `off_topic` (underscore → 422); (3) the interaction-limit `expiry` enum is `six_months`, not `6_months` (→ 422). Each propagated end-to-end into code, docs, the test suite, and the QA expectations.
- **target_failure:** a GitHub-REST-action spec authored with unverified HTTP methods / enum spellings, caught only at live-run time (rounds burn on 404/422) — and the offline mock silently masks them because it mirrors whatever the plan assumed.
- **guardrail:** For any spec that implements a GitHub-REST action, verify the exact HTTP method AND every enum value against the live GitHub REST API/docs (never the plan prose). The orchestrator should execute run-once repo actions (e.g. `hardening-lock-open-issues`, `interaction-limit`) ONCE against the target BEFORE the tester round, so API-contract defects (404/422) are fixed within the same implementation round instead of costing a tester FAIL. A lock failure returning 404 (not 403) is a method/route defect, not a permissions wedge — route the architect to fix the contract, not to grant permissions.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (API-contract verification) + playbooks/self-improver.md (deployment-caught defect discipline) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-03, #2798 implementation round 1) — re-observed on #2798: the `close-dependabot-prs` action's author filter used the GraphQL-normalized `app/dependabot`, but the raw REST `/pulls` list reports `user.login = dependabot[bot]`, so the live filter skipped all 13 PRs. Caught by the SI running the action once before the tester round (exactly the G-097 discipline); fixed the filter to match both forms + corrected the harness mock to seed the real REST value. The offline mock had mirrored the plan's assumed value, masking the defect until live-run — confirming the "run the action against the target before the tester round" rule is what surfaces contract defects.

### G-098: qa_brief_must_seed_durable_suite_files
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-03
- **observed:** #2797 planning — the QA Expert authored the `### QA Plan` + `**Feature tests:** pipeline-hygiene` line, but the dispatch brief did NOT instruct it to author the four durable suite files. The `planning → implementation` transition's auto `tests-commit` then had nothing to persist (no NOT-persisted note surfaced — the G-080 class silent swallow), and the suite was absent until the SI re-dispatched the QA Expert to seed it.
- **target_failure:** a QA brief that declares a `**Feature tests:**` feature domain but does not author the four `.opencode/tests/<feature>/*.md` files, so `tests-commit` persists nothing and the durable regression coverage is missing.
- **guardrail:** When a QA Expert brief names a `**Feature tests:**` domain, explicitly instruct it to author the four durable suite files (`functional.md`/`regression.md`/`exploratory.md`/`smoke.md`) in the same pass as the QA Plan — and if the folder already exists, EXTEND it (never regenerate — G-093/G-024). The SI should directory-check the declared suite folders when reviewing the plan; a mandated suite that is absent is a brief gap, not a silent `tests-commit` no-op.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (planning cluster brief) + playbooks/qa-expert.md (seed step) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** **Partial** (re-validated 2026-09-05, #2810) — the brief gap recurred a third time: the #2810 planning brief instructed the QA Expert to "declare `**Feature tests:** <feature-name>`" but did NOT tell it to author the four `.opencode/tests/window-engine-cleanup/*.md` files, so the transition's `tests-commit` no-op'd and the SI had to seed the suite before the tester entry. The G-098 fallback (SI seeds) worked, but the SI's planning-cluster brief is still not naming the four files explicitly. (Prior: #2807 si re-dispatch to seed; #2797 brief lacked the instruction.) The brief MUST name the four files for the QA Expert to author in the same pass. Re-validated (2026-09-06, #2826): the brief told the QA Expert to "declare `**Feature tests:** <name>`" (and create-only-if-missing), not to EXTEND the existing `launcher` suite with the new #2826 grid-de-dup regression rows — so the durable suite still lacks the dedup cases (they live in the QA Plan + the developer's vitest suite `featureRegistry.dedupe.test.tsx`). The `tests-commit` persisted the (unchanged) existing suite, and no round was burned, but the durability gap is real. The brief MUST distinguish EXTEND (folder exists — append the new rows to `regression.md`/`functional.md`) from create-only-if-missing. Re-validated under a FOURTH VARIANT (2026-09-08, #2838): the brief OVER-CORRECTED — it explicitly forbade the QA Expert from writing `.opencode/tests/**` ("your deliverable is restricted to the A2A edit"), so the drafted #2838 suite rows sat in the A2A section and the durable `window-manager` suite would have kept the stale #2821 F-16..F-19 contract at `tests-commit`. Caught at plan review and fixed by a re-dispatch instructing EXTEND. New explicit rule (see G-116): a QA brief that declares `**Feature tests:**` domains MUST instruct the QA to physically EXTEND the durable files and must NEVER forbid suite-file writes.

### G-099: audit_reads_config_static_plan_as_live
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-03
- **observed:** #2797 audit and #2798 audit — the audit's `verification_status` reads the plan policy by line-anchoring `> **Verification policy: static**`; the posted QA section rendered `static** — <prose>` (a bold close followed by prose on the same line), so it fell through to the `live` default. `verification_ok` then passed on `live_evidence` = a false-positive `telemetry_spans` scan of the explain-why-not justification ("No `telemetry_spans` surface exists for this config-only diff"). #2798 logged `verification_policy: live` + `live_telemetry_evidence: true` for a config-only static plan.
- **target_failure:** a config-only `static` plan is read as `live` by the audit (parsing the punctuation-rendered policy), and the verification gate passes on a false-positive telemetry-spans string rather than correctly gating on the PASS verdict.
- **guardrail:** The audit's plan-policy parser should tolerate `static` with or without trailing punctuation/bold/prose (`static**`, `static.**`, `static** — ...`, `static`) so a config-only `static` plan is read as static; the `live_evidence` telemetry-spans scan must not count the phrase in a negating/explain-why-not sentence. Gate `verification_ok` on the policy correctly (static → PASS verdict; live → PASS + genuine telemetry evidence). Hardened in #2798: the parser trims trailing `*`/`.`/`)`/`]` from the declared value token, and `live_evidence` is line-scoped with a `line_is_telemetry_negation` guard (harness 97/97).
- **home:** pipeline-state.rs (plan-policy parser + live-evidence scan — hardened in the SI's domain) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Partial (2026-09-06, #2830 audit) — the `line_is_telemetry_negation` guard did NOT fully exclude this run's negating phrasing: the pure-rendering plan's QA note "**There is NO `telemetry_spans` leg**" and "do NOT invent one" was reflected in the audit's `live_telemetry_evidence: true` field (the only `telemetry_spans` mention in the plan). `verification_ok` still passed (via G-108's rendered-webview path, not the telemetry scan), so no false block occurred — but the informational `live_telemetry_evidence` field is still counting a negation mention, so the guard's phrasing coverage is incomplete. (Prior: Confirmed 2026-09-03, #2798 audit — hardened the parser + live-evidence scan in pipeline-state.rs, harness 97/97.) Re-validated (2026-09-08, #2842 audit) — a second pure-rendering spec whose QA section declared `**Verification policy: live**` with the note "There is NO `telemetry_spans` query — theming emits no telemetry, so one MUST NOT be fabricated" logged `live_telemetry_evidence: true` from that negation mention, confirming the same incomplete coverage. `verification_ok` correctly passed via G-108 (rendered-webview receipts), so no false block — but the informational field is still polluted. The `line_is_telemetry_negation` guard's phrasing coverage remains incomplete: it must also exclude "NO `telemetry_spans` query", "one MUST NOT be fabricated", and "does NOT apply" — candidate phrasing set for the next hardening + harness check.

### G-103: compat_surface_rewire_must_cover_write_path
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-05
- **observed:** #2807 round 1 — an implementation slice replaced the third-party `@maomaolabs/core` window engine with an own kernel. Sub-task ST-5 ("preserve feature-caller + toolbar compatibility") re-pointed the toolbar's `useWindows` read to the own kernel but did NOT re-point the launcher OPEN dispatch, which still went through the third-party `Toolbar`'s internal store (whose `WindowManager` had been unmounted). Result: clicking a launcher opened NO window (AC2 FAIL, the round-1 only failure). The tester diagnosed the third-party `Toolbar` **ignores an `openWindow` prop** and hard-wires launcher `onClick` to its own `openWindow`, so the only correct route was to intercept the click (an `onClickCapture` wrapper) or lift the own opener up to the caller and pass it down — which the Architect's fix plan did.
- **target_failure:** a "compat surface" (or component-migration) sub-task that re-points a third-party component to an own kernel verifies only the data/read path and misses the WRITE/action path, so the migrated component silently dispatches through the third-party engine (whose host was unmounted) → the feature is unreachable and a live AC fails.
- **guardrail:** When a sub-task re-points a third-party component to an own kernel, enumerate and verify EVERY interaction path (read AND write/action dispatch), not only the data read. A third-party component that hard-wires its internal store dispatch cannot be re-pointed by a prop — verify this first (read the vendored component's source for how the onClick/openWindow is wired), then intercept the click (`onClickCapture` + `preventDefault`/`stopPropagation` at an ancestor container) or lift the own handler up to the caller and pass it down. The plan must name the write path (the launcher onClick → own openWindow) explicitly, and the developer's verification must confirm the click reaches the own kernel, not merely that the import source was swapped.
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (compat-surface decomposition — enumerate read + write paths) + playbooks/developer.md (verification — confirm the action reaches the own kernel) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-104: theme_semantic_token_shadowed_by_default_config
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-05
- **observed:** #2807 round 2 — the new window chrome header used `bg="bg.subtle"` and the icon tile used `bg="bg.muted"` (declared in `system.ts` to resolve to `var(--header-bg)` / `var(--card-hover-bg)`), but at runtime they resolved to Chakra `defaultConfig` built-ins (`#fafafa` / `#f4f4f5`) instead of the theme vars, making the light window title light-on-light / near-zero-contrast in BOTH theme presets (AC3 live FAIL). The surface bg (`bg.surface`), accent border (`accent.default`), and brand cap re-tinted correctly — the failure was isolated to exactly the two token keys that collide with Chakra built-ins. Root cause: `bg.subtle`/`bg.muted` are Chakra v3 `defaultConfig` built-in semantic tokens; a bare `{ value: 'var(..)' }` redefinition does not override a built-in that carries its own conditional definition. The whole rest of the app (~35 call sites) avoids this by referencing the theme CSS vars DIRECTLY (`var(--header-bg)`, `var(--card-hover-bg)`, `var(--border-color)`) — the established Fredo pattern.
- **target_failure:** a new themed component uses a Chakra semantic-token shorthand (`bg.subtle`/`bg.muted`) that collides with `defaultConfig` built-ins, so it renders at the built-in value instead of the theme CSS var, producing a broken/unreadable surface that AC3's token-native contract rejects.
- **guardrail:** In new themed components, reference the theme CSS vars DIRECTLY (`var(--header-bg)`, `var(--card-hover-bg)`, `var(--border-color)`) rather than the `bg.subtle`/`bg.muted` Chakra semantic-token shorthand — those two keys collide with Chakra `defaultConfig` built-ins and resolve to `#fafafa`/`#f4f4f5` instead of the theme vars. Use the Fredo custom tokens (`bg.surface`, `bg.canvas`, `accent.*`, `border.default`, `fg.*`) or the explicit `var()` form. When adding a themed surface, verify its computed color against the active theme (not just the source token) before calling it token-native. (Follow-up backlog: repair/remove the two broken `bg.subtle`/`bg.muted` definitions in `system.ts`, which also break `RunCliLaunchStatus.tsx`.)
- **home:** playbooks/software-architect.md (theming-contract token selection) + playbooks/tester.md (AC3 token-native verification — check the RUNNING computed color) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending
- **re-validated:** 2026-09-21, #2924 — the window chrome kept the direct theme-CSS-var pattern (no `bg.subtle`/`bg.muted` code props) and the live re-tint leg passed on three presets (Dark, Terminal Green, Light Default): header/tile/border resolved to the active theme vars and the title stayed readable; the collision did not recur.

### G-114: visual_comparison_gate_blocked_by_text_only_harness
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-08
- **observed:** #2837 round 1 — AC-1 mandated a visual/perceptual comparison gate (overlay the rendered avatar against a reference HTML/PNG wireframe and read the result by eye). Every role in `opencode.json` was pinned to `commandcode/deepseek-v4-flash`, which has NO image input (empirically confirmed by the tester AND the SI: PNG Read returns "Cannot read image"). The round-1 tester honestly marked the leg UNVERIFIED and FAILed rather than fabricating a PASS (correct per the plan's own QA-2837-1 rule), and the whole round blocked as an environment blocker — a code-only/DOM-only PASS was correctly forbidden.
- **target_failure:** an acceptance criterion that requires a perceptual visual comparison (reading reference images / judging a rendered surface by eye) meets a harness whose every agent role runs a text-only model — the gate cannot clear by any agent, a re-dispatch reproduces the same UNVERIFIED, and looping to implementation would author a fix for a nonexistent defect.
- **guardrail (RESOLVED 2026-09-17):** The harness is now vision-capable — every role runs an image-input model — so a mandatory visual/perceptual comparison AC can be judged directly by the tester (and a design-role vision read). Retained as a fallback: if a visual gate ever meets a non-vision harness again, (1) have the tester execute every NON-visual assertion to a coordinate-exact receipt (a live rendered-DOM/geometry overlay equal to the reference is strictly stronger than an eyeball for geometry) and upload real screenshots as the visual receipts; (2) record the exact named blocker per G-053 and route the perceptual leg to the human as a G-050 PO acceptance (a Status comment is a valid gate-clearing path); (3) do NOT loop `testing → implementation` for this class (no code defect) — reconcile via the recorded human acceptance + round advance. For exact pixel deltas, always pair the vision read with measured rendered geometry (G-154), never the vision read alone.
- **home:** references.md (this record) + playbooks/self-improver.md (escalation routing for vision gates)
- **effectiveness:** **Resolved** (2026-09-17) — the text-only harness that caused this (every role pinned to a model whose PNG Read returned "Cannot read image") no longer exists; all roles are now vision-capable, so a visual comparison gate is judged directly instead of routing to the human.

### G-115: pass_verdict_line_must_not_contain_fail_substring
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-08
- **observed:** #2837 rounds 2-3 — the round-2 corrected verdict posted as `Verdict: **PASS** — supersedes the prior round's FAIL verdict. ...` and the `testing → audit` transition was REFUSED with "tester verdict is not PASS": the machine's whole-line parser (`pipeline-state.rs:2200-2206`, `verdict_pass = line.contains("pass") && !line.contains("fail")`) read the "FAIL" inside the supersession clause and classified the PASS verdict as FAIL. Comments are immutable and G-020 forbids a second verdict per round, so the only correction was a full round-3 re-entry (`testing → implementation → testing`) re-posting the same evidence with a clean standalone `Verdict: **PASS**` line — one round burned on a drafting nuance.
- **target_failure:** a PASS verdict whose `Verdict:` line carries the word "fail"/"FAIL" anywhere on it (a supersession clause, a "no failed ACs" cross-reference, a quoted prior verdict) is mechanically parsed as FAIL by the whole-line verdict predicate, refusing the audit transition and costing a re-entry round.
- **guardrail:** The `Verdict:` line in a `## Tests Runs` draft MUST be a standalone line containing ONLY the verdict token (e.g. `Verdict: **PASS**`) with the word "fail"/"FAIL" nowhere on that line — all context (supersession explanation, prior-round disposition, cross-references) goes in a separate paragraph BELOW the verdict line. Applies to every verdict the tester or SI drafts, PASS or FAIL (a FAIL verdict line must similarly avoid ambiguity). A PASS verdict whose line mentions a prior FAIL should be rewritten with the explanation below the line.
- **home:** references.md (this record) + playbooks/tester.md (Tests Runs drafting — standalone verdict line) + the Tests-runs comment template
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-116: qa_brief_must_never_forbid_durable_suite_writes
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-08
- **observed:** #2838 planning — the QA Expert dispatch brief OVER-CORRECTED the G-098 lesson by explicitly forbidding the QA Expert from writing `.opencode/tests/**` ("your deliverable is restricted to the A2A edit; do NOT write under `.opencode/tests/**`"). The QA Expert authored the full #2838 dock suite-row drafts (F-20..F-35, S-9, regression R-11/R-12, desktop-chrome F-14/R-15) inside its A2A section and even flagged the gap to the orchestrator, but the durable `window-manager` suite still carried the stale #2821 bottom-tray contract (F-16..F-19) when `tests-commit` was about to run — the tester would have failed the new dock against the wrong rows. Caught at plan review (QA's Discussion note) and fixed by a re-dispatch instructing the QA to physically EXTEND the five durable files.
- **target_failure:** a QA planning brief that restricts the QA Expert to A2A edits while its `**Feature tests:**` line declares durable suite domains — `tests-commit` then persists the pre-existing (stale/superseded) suite content, and the tester runs against the wrong rows.
- **guardrail:** A QA Expert dispatch brief that names `**Feature tests:**` domains MUST (a) name the four durable `.opencode/tests/<feature>/*.md` files, (b) instruct EXTEND (folder exists — append new rows; never regenerate, G-093/G-024), and (c) NEVER forbid suite-file writes — the QA Expert is the sole test author and its durable-suite edits are the `tests-commit` content. When the SI reviews the plan, directory-check the declared suite folders; a declared-but-unseeded domain or a forbidden-writes brief is a brief gap (G-098 class), not a silent `tests-commit` no-op.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (planning-cluster QA brief) + references.md (this record, sibling of G-098)
- **effectiveness:** Partial (2026-09-09, #2848 planning) — the lesson REPEATED despite the record: the #2848 QA-Expert dispatch brief again told the QA Expert NOT to create the durable suite files ("the actual files are persisted by the transition/tests-commit; do not create them in this phase"), re-deriving the exact G-098/G-116 over-correction. The declared `app-dock` domain was left unseeded, and the SI had to resume the QA session a SECOND time to physically create the four files before the transition. The rule is still not baked where the SI composes QA briefs (playbooks/self-improver.md step 2 does not yet state it). Strengthen: the SI planning-cluster QA brief template must carry the G-116 (a)/(b)/(c) clause verbatim.

### G-117: empty_developer_dispatch_retry_by_resume_first
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-08
- **observed:** #2838 implementation — the DEVELOPER role returned two consecutive EMPTY final reports with zero work (no commit on `spec/2838`, clean worktree) on a model shared with all working sibling roles (`commandcode/deepseek-v4-flash` is every agent's model in `opencode.json`). Per G-048's letter this looked like a role-model outage, but the model is shared, so a role-specific provider regression was implausible. The THIRD dispatch — a resume of the second session via `task_id` — produced the complete implementation + push in one pass. The empty fresh dispatches may share a harness-level transient that a resumed session avoids (prior context + warm session).
- **target_failure:** treating two consecutive EMPTY developer dispatches on a SHARED model as a G-048 role-model outage and blocking/escalating to the human, when the resolution was a session-resume re-dispatch that completed the work.
- **guardrail:** When a subagent role returns empty dispatches on a model SHARED with working sibling roles (check `opencode.json`: distinct model per role is the G-048 precondition), do NOT block/escalate after two empties — retry by RESUMING the last session (`task_id`) with the full brief restated; the resume path completed the work in the observed case. Reserve the G-048 block/escalate path for (a) a role with a UNIQUE model assignment that fails, or (b) empty dispatches that persist across resumed-session retries too. Check the branch/worktree state (commit present? worktree dirty?) before re-dispatching so an empty report is not misread as no-work when the push may have landed.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (step 8 model-availability triage — add resume-first) + references.md (this record, sibling of G-048)
- **effectiveness:** Partial (2026-09-09, #2848 ST-3) — the resume-first path was TRIED and did NOT resolve this instance: Dev B's session was resumed once (returned empty again), then two FRESH sessions also returned empty (4 total empties), while a trivial developer-role probe SUCCEEDED (proving the role/model worked) and sibling ST-1/2/4 dispatches succeeded. The actual resolution was DECOMPOSING the monolithic ST-3 brief (a ~400-line subtle state machine + dense multi-part instructions + tests) into two smaller concrete dispatches (ST-3a geometry/render, ST-3b predicates/roving/tests), which each completed first-pass. This run adds a distinct antecedent to G-117: an empty-dispatch streak on a task whose brief is LARGE + DENSE is more likely a brief-complexity limit than a session/harness transient — retry by splitting the brief into small concrete dispatches, not by blind resume/re-dispatch. Reserve resume-first for streaks on NORMAL-SIZED briefs.

### G-118: optional_chain_index_and_stale_closure_batch_defects
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-08
- **observed:** #2845 round 1 — the round-1 implementation introduced TWO deterministic code-level defects that each cost the tester a FAIL and a full round-2 rework. (1) **Optional-chain index throw:** `ThemingSettings.tsx` `<activePreset>.colors` was optional-chained (`activePreset?.colors`) but the `[key]` INDEX was applied to the (possibly `undefined`) result — `(activePreset?.colors as ...)[key]` — so when `activePreset` is `null` (the product's DEFAULT no-preset state, or Reset → `selectedPreset === ''`), the `[key]` access threw `Cannot read properties of undefined` BEFORE the `?? base` fallback on the next line could run, unmounting the whole Appearance editor (F-7/R-2/R-1). The optional chain guards the PROPERTY but not the INDEX. (2) **Stale-closure batch multi-write:** `handleDiscard` looped `dirtyTokens.forEach((key) => setOverride(key, ''))`; each `setOverride` rebuilds `{ ...overrides }` from the SAME render closure and calls a plain-value setter (no functional updater), so under React event-handler batching only the LAST iteration's deletion is committed and N-1 dirty keys survive as overrides (F-9 multi-edit).
- **target_failure:** a resolver/derivation author writes `<obj>?.prop[key]` (optional chain on the property, then a bare index), silently throwing before a `??` fallback when the object is nullish — and/or loops a plain-value setter over multiple keys inside one event handler, losing all but the last write under batching. Both are latent-only-when-a-specific-state-exists (no preset; multi-token batch), so unit tests of the happy path never catch them; the DEFAULT state and the multi-edit path are exactly what a live tester hits.
- **guardrail:** (1) When a resolve path has a nullish object followed by an index, optional-chain the INDEX as well: use `obj?.prop?.[key]` (or narrow the cast first: `(obj?.prop as T | undefined)?.[key]`), so a nullish obj short-circuits to `undefined` and the `?? fallback` on the next line runs instead of throwing. Verify the DEFAULT/null state renders (not just a selected state). (2) When clearing/persisting MULTIPLE keys in one event handler, compose ONE next object and write it ONCE — never loop a plain-value setter over keys (a stale-closure batch drops all but the last). Prefer a bulk writer (`clearOverrides(keys)`) or a functional updater over the prior state. The developer's CI-parity build/typecheck does NOT guard these (both are runtime-typed-correct); the SI's step-7 review should spot-check a resolver's null-path handling and a batch-write's single-compose before the tester round.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (step-7 review — spot-check resolver null-paths + batch-write single-compose) + references.md (this record, sibling of G-109/G-111 claim-vs-evidence family)
- **effectiveness:** Confirmed (2026-09-08, #2845 round 1→2) — the round-2 re-test confirmed BOTH defects fixed: `activePreset?.colors?.[key]` (guarded index) rendered base values on Default/None and Reset with no crash (F-7/R-2/R-1 PASS), and the new `clearOverrides([...dirtyTokens])` atomic single-write reverted ALL dirty tokens in one gesture (F-9 multi-edit PASS). One round for the total fix, no further retry.

### G-119: tests_commit_overwrites_main_with_stale_spec_branch_suite
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-09
- **observed:** #2848 round 2 — the tester's first `tests-commit` re-pushed the SPEC-BRANCH copy of `.opencode/tests/app-dock/` (still the unchecked round-0 seed) over `main`, silently dropping the round-1 `[x]` PASS annotations that main's copy carried (the round-1 tester had marked F-rows pass/fail and persisted them). The tester caught it via `git show origin/main:<file>`, reconstructed all four suite files preserving round-1 PASS + round-2 updates, and re-committed — but the overwrite is a latent data-loss foot-gun: `tests-commit` does not diff/merge, it replaces `main`'s suite with whatever is in the working tree, and the spec branch's suite copy can be stale vs `main` (the branch forked before later suite-persistence on main).
- **target_failure:** a tester's `tests-commit` persists a STALE suite copy (spec-branch seed, pre-round annotations) over `main`'s richer current suite, silently destroying accumulated per-round PASS/FAIL evidence because tests-commit overwrites rather than diffs/merges.
- **guardrail:** Before a tester runs `tests-commit`, it MUST base its suite edits on `origin/main`'s CURRENT suite content — diff the working-tree/spec-branch copy against `origin/main`'s copy of each `.opencode/tests/<feature>/*.md` file first (read via `git show origin/main:<path>`), and reconcile any divergence so the `main` version's accumulated annotations are preserved, never clobbered by a stale seed. `tests-commit` replaces whole files; treat it as a write of the RECONCILED content, not a blind push of the working tree. (Pipeline hardening candidate: make tests-commit diff/merge against `origin/main` or refuse when the spec-branch copy is strictly older.)
- **home:** playbooks/tester.md (suite-persistence step — reconcile vs origin/main before tests-commit) + references.md (this record)
- **effectiveness:** Pending

### G-120: empty_dispatch_on_large_dense_brief_splits_not_redispatch
- **activation_date:** 2026-09-09
- **observed:** #2848 ST-3 implementation — FOUR consecutive empty developer dispatches (two fresh Dev-B sessions + a resume + a fresh Dev-A2 in a known-good worktree) on a LARGE, DENSE brief (parameterize a ~400-line subtle state machine across geometry/predicates/roving/tests with many non-goal invariants), while (a) a trivial developer-role probe returned correctly, (b) sibling ST-1/ST-2/ST-4 normal-sized briefs on the SAME role/model completed first-pass, and (c) NO work ever landed (clean worktrees each time). Splitting the brief into two SMALL concrete dispatches (ST-3a geometry+render; ST-3b predicates+roving+tests) completed both first-pass with full verification.
- **target_failure:** a developer dispatch whose brief is very large and dense (a big single multi-file refactor of a subtle module, many stacked instructions, long constraint lists) returns silent EMPTY final reports across repeated fresh/resumed sessions while the role demonstrably works on smaller briefs — blind re-dispatch and resume-first both waste rounds.
- **guardrail:** When a developer dispatch returns EMPTY on a LARGE/DENSE brief (multi-file refactor of a subtle module, long stacked instructions) while the role works on smaller briefs and trivial probes succeed, DECOMPOSE the brief into small concrete slices (each one file/concern, one clear deliverable, its own verification) and dispatch each separately — do NOT keep re-dispatching or resuming the monolithic brief. Reserve resume-first (G-117) and G-048 role-outage handling for normal-sized briefs. When staffing a plan, prefer planning sub-tasks small enough to be single-concern developer briefs from the start.
- **home:** playbooks/self-improver.md (step 6 staffing/dispatch — large developer briefs are decomposed before dispatch) + references.md (this record, sibling of G-117)
- **effectiveness:** **Confirmed** (2026-09-13, #2870) — the 5-pt multi-file ST-2 returned two empty developer dispatches (the full brief, then a smaller-but-still-multi-file slice) while the same developer role completed ST-1 and a minimal single-file slice; decomposing into single-concern slices (one prop / one extraction / one gating / one teleport-wiring), each with a SHORT brief, completed them all first-pass. Density — not role outage — was the cause, exactly as recorded.
